# Borbax V0 Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build a generated chemistry that runs in a well-mixed beaker, and prove that catalysis emerges from molecular shape alone — with nothing in the code that knows what an enzyme is.

**Architecture:** A Rust workspace of small, single-purpose crates. A 64-bit seed generates a periodic table with genuine periodicity; molecules are atom graphs reduced to a canonical form, embedded in 3D, and sampled along the vertices of a subdivided icosahedron into a directional shape signature; binding is a complementarity test between two signatures searched over the 60-element icosahedral rotation group; polymers fold on an FCC lattice and expose enclosed cavities; catalysis is two cavities sitting close enough to hold two reactants together. Everything expensive is computed once per *species*, never per molecule. No world and no spatial simulation — it all runs in a single well-mixed volume so the iteration loop stays measured in seconds — but shapes are rendered to SVG from the start, because a shape-based chemistry cannot be developed blind.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 1.97.1, pinned via proto. Settled crates rather than
hand-rolling — `thiserror`, `clap`, `libm`, `serde`, `rustc-hash` at runtime;
`proptest`, `insta`, `criterion`, `rstest` for tests. Vector maths and the graph algorithms
*are* written directly — because they are small, not because a crate would
break determinism. `glam` and `nalgebra` would both be bit-identical here
(checked in their source), so this is a size judgement. Stating it as a
determinism argument would be a false doctrine that later gets used to reject
a dependency that is genuinely correct. See CLAUDE.md § Dependencies for the rule
that decides which is which: dev-dependencies are cheap, a runtime dependency
in a result-affecting path is a physics change.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-26-borbax-prd.md`. V0 exit criteria are §23.

## Global Constraints

These apply to every task. A task's requirements implicitly include this section.

- **Rust 1.97.1**, pinned in `.prototools`. Never bump without regenerating golden hashes (spec §18.1).
- **Determinism is a hard requirement.** Same seed → bit-identical output, always. No `HashMap` iteration in any code path that affects results (use `BTreeMap`, or sort before iterating). No wall-clock. No thread-scheduling-dependent reduction order. No `f32`; all simulation arithmetic is `f64`.
- **All randomness comes from `borbax_rng::Stream`.** Never `rand::random`, never a thread-local RNG, never `std::collections::hash_map::RandomState`.
- **No real chemistry data anywhere** (spec §5, G1). No element tables, no reaction databases, no molecular structures. CI enforces this.
- **No real-world units** (G4). Temperature is `Thermal`, energy is `Quanta`, distance is `Span`, time is `WorldYear`, mass is `Mass`. These are distinct newtypes and the compiler enforces they do not mix.
- **Every reaction conserves mass.** Products' total mass equals reactants' total mass, exactly, checked in tests.
- **No `unwrap()` or `expect()` in library code.** Return `Result` or `Option`. Tests may unwrap only behind a module-level `#[allow(clippy::unwrap_used, reason = "...")]` — `clippy.toml` sets no `allow-unwrap-in-tests`, so the deny reaches inside `#[cfg(test)]` and an unadorned `.unwrap()` in a test fails `-D warnings`. Prefer avoiding the spelling: `assert!` for conditions, `unwrap_or_else(|| unreachable!("..."))` where a fixture guarantees the value.
- **Commit after every task.** Conventional commits (`feat:`, `test:`, `fix:`, `chore:`, `docs:`).
- **Every commit message ends with the trailer:** `MrReasonable <4990954+MrReasonable@users.noreply.github.com>`

## File Structure

```
borbax/
├── .prototools                      Toolchain pins
├── Cargo.toml                       Workspace root
├── rustfmt.toml                     Formatting config
├── .github/workflows/ci.yml         Test + lint + fiction-guarantee checks
├── xtask/                           Fiction-guarantee CI checks (no-data, blocklist, units)
│   └── src/main.rs
└── crates/
    ├── borbax-units/
    │   ├── src/lib.rs               Thermal, Quanta, Span, WorldYear, fixed-point Mass
    │   └── src/det_math.rs          Portable transcendentals (§13.1) — see note below
    ├── borbax-rng/
    │   └── src/lib.rs               Counter-based Stream, Domain enum
    ├── borbax-universe/
    │   ├── src/lib.rs               Universe struct, generate()
    │   ├── src/element.rs           Element, ElementId, shell pattern, property derivation
    │   ├── src/naming.rs            Phonotactic name generator + real-name blocklist
    │   └── src/bonds.rs             BondEnergyMatrix
    ├── borbax-molecule/
    │   ├── src/lib.rs               Re-exports
    │   ├── src/graph.rs             Molecule, Bond, atom graph
    │   ├── src/canonical.rs         Individualization-refinement canonical labelling
    │   ├── src/geodesic.rs          Subdivided-icosahedron directions, 60-rotation permutation table
    │   ├── src/layout.rs            Stress-majorization 3D embedding
    │   ├── src/signature.rs         Signature, directional sampling, rotation-canonicalisation
    │   ├── src/binding.rs           affinity(), rotation search, invariant pre-filter, memo
    │   ├── src/polymer.rs           Polymer, monomer sequence
    │   ├── src/fold.rs              FCC-lattice annealing, FoldWorkspace, FoldCache
    │   └── src/cavity.rs            Cavity detection, enclosure measure, local signatures
    ├── borbax-reaction/
    │   ├── src/lib.rs               Re-exports
    │   ├── src/kind.rs              ReactionKind, Reaction
    │   ├── src/rate.rs              Arrhenius rate law
    │   ├── src/decay.rs             Spontaneous cleavage + solvent attack
    │   ├── src/catalysis.rs         Two-cavity geometric enhancement
    │   └── src/raf.rs               Hordijk-Steel RAF detection
    ├── borbax-beaker/
    │   ├── src/lib.rs               Beaker, well-mixed Gillespie stepping
    │   ├── src/battery.rs           Universe validation criteria
    │   └── src/metrics.rs           Activity / novelty / decay metric families
    ├── borbax-render/
    │   ├── src/svg.rs               Minimal deterministic SVG writer
    │   ├── src/molecule.rs          Graph diagram + orthographic 3D projection
    │   ├── src/geodesic_net.rs      Signature as an unfolded icosahedral net
    │   ├── src/fold.rs              Isometric fold view, cavities, burial shading
    │   └── src/binding.rs           Binding pair in winning orientation
    ├── borbax-geometry/
    │   ├── src/project.rs           Orthographic + isometric projection, depth ordering
    │   ├── src/net.rs               Icosahedron unfolding for signature nets
    │   └── src/palette.rs           Colour mapping for affinity, extent, burial
    └── borbax-cli/
        └── src/main.rs              `beaker`, `sweep`, `battery`, `render`, `goldens`
```

`borbax-geometry` computes what to draw; `borbax-render` turns that into SVG. V1's
`borbax-ui` will be a second backend over the same geometry (spec §14.5), which is
why the split exists this early rather than being introduced later.

**No TypeScript, Node or pnpm** — the native-viewer decision (spec §14.0) removed
the entire JavaScript toolchain from the project. One language, one build, one
test command.

Each crate has one responsibility and can be understood without reading its neighbours. `borbax-molecule` carries the conceptual weight of the project and is deliberately split into small files — most iteration will happen there.

**`det_math` is in `borbax-units` deliberately, and must stay there.** It is
called from `borbax-rng`'s normal-variate draw and `borbax-universe`'s mass
generation, both of which sit *below* `borbax-molecule` in the crate order. Put
it any higher and the one file that exists to guarantee portability is
unreachable from two of the crates that need it, with the only escape being a
dependency edge that inverts the whole ordering. `borbax-units` has no
dependencies of its own, so it is the one place every crate can reach.

## Phase 1 — Foundation

### Task 1: Workspace, toolchain, and fiction-guarantee CI

**Files:**
- Create: `.prototools`, `Cargo.toml`, `rustfmt.toml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
- Create: `xtask/Cargo.toml`, `xtask/src/main.rs`
- Test: `xtask/src/main.rs` (self-testing binary)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing
- Produces: a workspace where `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo run -p xtask -- check-guarantees` both succeed

- [x] **Step 1: Create the toolchain pin**

```toml
# .prototools — toolchain pins. These are part of the determinism
# contract (spec §13.1, §18.1), not just developer convenience. A rustc
# bump can change floating-point codegen and silently invalidate the
# golden-run hashes; bumping any pin here requires deliberate golden
# regeneration, reviewed like a change to physics.

# Current stable, released 2026-07-14.
# renovate: datasource=github-releases depName=rust-lang/rust
rust = "1.97.1"

[settings]
auto-install = true
```

Rust is the only entry. The native-viewer decision (spec §14.0) removed Node,
pnpm and TypeScript from the project entirely — one language, one build, one
test command.

Verify the pin is current rather than trusting the value above:

```bash
curl -s https://static.rust-lang.org/dist/channel-rust-stable.toml \
  | grep -m1 -A2 '^\[pkg.rust\]' | grep version
```

- [x] **Step 2: Create the workspace root**

```toml
# Cargo.toml
[workspace]
resolver = "3"
# `crates/*` joins this list in Task 2, with the first crate. Cargo falls back
# to treating a glob that matches nothing as a literal path, so listing
# `crates/*` before `crates/` has a package in it fails the workspace outright.
members = ["xtask"]

[workspace.package]
edition = "2024"
rust-version = "1.97.1"
license = "MIT"

[workspace.dependencies]
borbax-units = { path = "crates/borbax-units" }
borbax-rng = { path = "crates/borbax-rng" }
borbax-universe = { path = "crates/borbax-universe" }
borbax-molecule = { path = "crates/borbax-molecule" }
borbax-reaction = { path = "crates/borbax-reaction" }
borbax-beaker = { path = "crates/borbax-beaker" }
borbax-render = { path = "crates/borbax-render" }
borbax-geometry = { path = "crates/borbax-geometry" }

[workspace.dependencies.thiserror]
version = "2"
[workspace.dependencies.libm]
version = "0.2"

[workspace.lints.rust]
# No unsafe anywhere. If a benchmark ever justifies it, lift this on the one
# crate that needs it rather than the workspace — the exception should be
# visible in a diff.
unsafe_code = "forbid"
missing_docs = "warn"
missing_debug_implementations = "warn"
unreachable_pub = "warn"
rust_2018_idioms = { level = "warn", priority = -1 }

[workspace.lints.rustdoc]
broken_intra_doc_links = "deny"

[workspace.lints.clippy]
# Groups at priority -1 so the specific lints below can override them.
pedantic = { level = "warn", priority = -1 }
nursery = { level = "warn", priority = -1 }
cargo = { level = "warn", priority = -1 }

# Denied because in this codebase they are correctness, not style. A panic in
# a library is a simulation that dies eight hours into an overnight run.
unwrap_used = "deny"
expect_used = "deny"
panic = "deny"
todo = "deny"
unimplemented = "deny"

# `a[i]` is an unwrap wearing a hat. Warn rather than deny because the
# geometry code indexes fixed-size arrays with proven bounds constantly —
# those take an #[allow] with a one-line reason, and a bare #[allow] is a
# review finding.
indexing_slicing = "warn"

# Silent truncation is the gotcha most likely to produce a wrong number that
# looks plausible. Prefer TryFrom.
as_conversions = "warn"
cast_possible_truncation = "warn"
cast_precision_loss = "warn"
cast_sign_loss = "warn"

# Allowed, with reasons — a bare allow is not acceptable anywhere else.
# `borbax_molecule::MoleculeGraph` reads better than the alternative.
module_name_repetitions = "allow"
# r/a and x/y/z are this domain's actual vocabulary, not lazy naming.
similar_names = "allow"
# #[must_use] is applied deliberately where dropping the result is a bug,
# not exhaustively on every getter.
must_use_candidate = "allow"

[profile.release]
lto = "thin"
codegen-units = 1

# The units newtypes (Task 2) scalarise to bare f64 in release, but in debug
# every operator is a real function call — and the beaker harness's whole
# value is that a chemistry-tuning iteration takes seconds. Without this,
# that claim only holds under --release and the test suite is unpleasant.
[profile.dev]
opt-level = 1

[profile.dev.package."*"]
opt-level = 2
```

```toml
# rustfmt.toml
#
# Deliberately close to defaults. rustfmt's defaults *are* the industry
# standard, and every deviation is a thing every contributor has to learn.
# `max_width = 100` is restated rather than changed — it is already rustfmt's
# default, and it is written down here so that the sub-widths derived from it
# (`array_width` and `fn_call_width` are 60% of it) are traceable to something
# visible when they wrap a line shorter than 100.
edition = "2024"
max_width = 100

# The one deviation that is not cosmetic. The default is `Auto`, which means
# "whatever the file already uses" — so a file first written on Windows keeps
# CRLF, `cargo fmt --check` stays green on the machine that wrote it, and the
# Windows leg of the determinism matrix (spec §13.4) reads different bytes for
# the same source. Pinning to Unix makes line endings one of the things that
# cannot vary by platform.
newline_style = "Unix"

use_field_init_shorthand = true
use_try_shorthand = true
```

Note that `group_imports` and `imports_granularity` — the two options most
worth having — are still nightly-only. Not worth a nightly toolchain when the
toolchain pin is part of the determinism contract (spec §18.1); import
grouping stays a review matter rather than an automated one.

Lints live in the workspace `Cargo.toml` above rather than in `lib.rs`
attributes, so a new crate inherits them by adding `[lints] workspace = true`
and cannot silently opt out.

Tests relax two of them. Determinism tests compare floats with exact `==` on
purpose, and every test unwraps freely — so test modules carry:

```rust
#![cfg_attr(test, allow(clippy::float_cmp, clippy::unwrap_used, clippy::indexing_slicing))]
```

- [x] **Step 3: Write the failing fiction-guarantee check**

`xtask/Cargo.toml`:

```toml
[package]
name = "xtask"
edition.workspace = true
# Inherited explicitly. `[workspace.package] rust-version` is not applied to a
# member unless the member asks for it, so omitting this line does not fall
# back to the workspace value — it means the crate has no MSRV at all, and
# cargo will build it with any toolchain. In a repo where the pin is part of
# the determinism contract (spec §18.1), that is the difference between a
# stale toolchain failing loudly and silently producing different floats.
rust-version.workspace = true
version = "0.1.0"

[dependencies]
```

**Every crate created from Task 2 onward needs `rust-version.workspace = true`.**
Verify with `cargo metadata --no-deps | jq '.packages[] | {name, rust_version}'`
— a `null` there means that crate will build on any toolchain, silently. The
guard is easy to confirm: forcing an older toolchain must fail with
`error: rustc <old> is not supported by the following package`.

`xtask/src/main.rs`:

```rust
//! Fiction-guarantee enforcement (spec §5). Run in CI.
//!
//! These checks make "no real chemistry" an architectural property rather
//! than a promise. They are cheap and they run on every commit.

use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

/// Extensions that would indicate bulk data smuggled into a chemistry crate.
/// The entire chemistry is generated from a seed; there is nothing legitimate
/// for a data file to be doing in these crates.
const DATA_EXTENSIONS: &[&str] = &["csv", "tsv", "json", "yaml", "yml", "parquet", "bin", "dat"];

/// Crates that must contain no data files at all (G1).
const CHEMISTRY_CRATES: &[&str] = &[
    "crates/borbax-universe",
    "crates/borbax-molecule",
    "crates/borbax-reaction",
];

/// Real chemical-format tokens that must never appear anywhere (G5).
const FORBIDDEN_FORMAT_TOKENS: &[&str] = &["SMILES", "InChI", "FASTA", "PDB format"];

fn main() -> Result<(), String> {
    let root = PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
        .parent()
        .ok_or("no workspace root")?
        .to_path_buf();

    let mut failures = Vec::new();
    check_no_data_files(&root, &mut failures)?;
    check_blocklist_present(&root, &mut failures)?;

    if failures.is_empty() {
        println!("fiction guarantees: all checks passed");
        Ok(())
    } else {
        for f in &failures {
            eprintln!("FAIL: {f}");
        }
        Err(format!(
            "{} fiction-guarantee check(s) failed",
            failures.len()
        ))
    }
}

/// G1 — no real chemistry data enters the repository.
fn check_no_data_files(root: &Path, failures: &mut Vec<String>) -> Result<(), String> {
    for krate in CHEMISTRY_CRATES {
        let dir = root.join(krate);
        if !dir.exists() {
            continue;
        }
        for entry in walk(&dir)? {
            let Some(ext) = entry.extension().and_then(|e| e.to_str()) else {
                continue;
            };
            if DATA_EXTENSIONS.contains(&ext) {
                failures.push(format!(
                    "G1: data file in chemistry crate: {}",
                    entry.display()
                ));
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// G2 — the blocklist must exist and be non-trivial, or name generation
/// is unguarded and could mint a real element symbol.
fn check_blocklist_present(root: &Path, failures: &mut Vec<String>) -> Result<(), String> {
    let path = root.join("crates/borbax-universe/src/naming.rs");
    if !path.exists() {
        // Not yet written — Task 4 creates it. Not a failure before then.
        return Ok(());
    }
    let src = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    if !src.contains("REAL_ELEMENT_SYMBOLS") {
        failures.push("G2: naming.rs has no REAL_ELEMENT_SYMBOLS blocklist".into());
    }
    for token in FORBIDDEN_FORMAT_TOKENS {
        if src.contains(token) {
            failures.push(format!("G5: forbidden format token {token:?} in naming.rs"));
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn walk(dir: &Path) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, String> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    let mut stack = vec![dir.to_path_buf()];
    while let Some(d) = stack.pop() {
        for entry in std::fs::read_dir(&d).map_err(|e| e.to_string())? {
            let path = entry.map_err(|e| e.to_string())?.path();
            if path.is_dir() {
                if path.file_name().and_then(|n| n.to_str()) != Some("target") {
                    stack.push(path);
                }
            } else {
                out.push(path);
            }
        }
    }
    // Sort so failures are reported in a stable order.
    out.sort();
    Ok(out)
}
```

- [x] **Step 4: Run the check and verify it passes on an empty tree**

Run: `proto install && cargo run -p xtask -- check-guarantees`
Expected: `fiction guarantees: all checks passed`

- [x] **Step 5: Prove the check actually bites**

Run:
```bash
mkdir -p crates/borbax-universe/src && echo '{}' > crates/borbax-universe/elements.json
cargo run -p xtask
```
Expected: `FAIL: G1: data file in chemistry crate: .../elements.json` and a non-zero exit.

Then remove it: `rm crates/borbax-universe/elements.json`

A check that has never been observed to fail is not a check. This step exists so that observation happens once, deliberately.

- [x] **Step 6: Add CI**

```yaml
# .github/workflows/ci.yml
name: CI
on: [push, pull_request]

jobs:
  # The matrix is not for coverage — it is the enforcement mechanism for the
  # cross-platform determinism promise in spec §13.4. Every portability
  # hazard listed there is invisible on a single machine: the code runs, the
  # tests pass, the numbers look fine, and the failure only appears when
  # someone opens a shared seed on different hardware. Running goldens on
  # one platform would pass happily while the promise quietly rotted.
  test:
    name: test (${{ matrix.os }})
    runs-on: ${{ matrix.os }}
    strategy:
      fail-fast: false
      matrix:
        os: [macos-latest, ubuntu-latest, windows-latest]
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v5
      - uses: moonrepo/setup-toolchain@v0
        with:
          auto-install: true
      - name: Fiction guarantees (spec §5)
        run: cargo run -p xtask
      - name: Format
        run: cargo fmt --all --check
      - name: Clippy
        run: cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings
      - name: Test
        run: cargo test --workspace
      # borbax-cli does not exist until Task 21. Without this guard the whole
      # `test` job fails on all three platforms for twenty tasks — and because
      # `determinism-matrix` is gated on `needs: test`, it would never run at
      # all. Its inertness would be accidental rather than designed, which is
      # the opposite of what §13.6 needs.
      #
      # `shell: bash` is load-bearing, not tidiness. The Windows leg would
      # otherwise run under pwsh, whose redirection operator rewrites the
      # stream with native CRLF line endings — so `diff -u` in
      # `determinism-matrix` would report a divergence on the very first
      # golden run, for a reason that has nothing to do with the simulation.
      # A determinism gate that cries wolf once gets muted forever.
      - name: Emit golden hashes
        if: hashFiles('crates/borbax-cli/Cargo.toml') != ''
        shell: bash
        run: cargo run -p borbax-cli --release -- goldens --emit > goldens-${{ matrix.os }}.txt
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        if: hashFiles('crates/borbax-cli/Cargo.toml') != ''
        with:
          name: goldens-${{ matrix.os }}
          path: goldens-${{ matrix.os }}.txt

  # The actual cross-platform assertion. Task 1 creates the job with a
  # placeholder step; it becomes real once borbax-cli exists (Task 20).
  determinism-matrix:
    name: goldens identical across platforms
    needs: test
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/download-artifact@v4
      - name: Compare golden hashes across platforms
        shell: bash
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          shopt -s nullglob
          files=(goldens-*/goldens-*.txt)
          if [ ${#files[@]} -eq 0 ]; then
            echo "No golden artifacts yet (expected before Task 20)."
            exit 0
          fi
          ref="${files[0]}"
          fail=0
          for f in "${files[@]:1}"; do
            if ! diff -u "$ref" "$f"; then
              echo "DETERMINISM FAILURE: $f differs from $ref"
              fail=1
            fi
          done
          [ $fail -eq 0 ] && echo "goldens identical across ${#files[@]} platforms"
          exit $fail
```

Note `macos-latest` is aarch64 and the other two are x86-64, so the matrix
covers both architectures as well as all three operating systems.

**The golden state hash has to be specified, not assumed.** It is the mechanism
the whole §13.6 contract rests on, and "emit a hash" is not a specification.
Task 21 implements it to this shape:

- a fixed list of seeds and checkpoint step counts, committed alongside the code
- species emitted in `SpeciesId` order — never map order
- counts as `borbax_units::canonical_bits`, `Mass` as `canonical_bits()`, time as `canonical_bits` — **not** `f64::to_bits`, which leaves the architecture-dependent NaN sign and the `-0.0`/`0.0` distinction in the hash. Assert finiteness at this boundary as well: canonicalising is belt-and-braces, and on its own it would turn a genuine NaN divergence into a green matrix
- fed to a fixed digest, never `DefaultHasher` (whose output is not stable
  across Rust releases)

Both the ordering and the bit-exact float encoding are load-bearing. A hash that
formats floats as decimal strings would hide exactly the divergences this exists
to catch.

### Task 2: borbax-units — type-safe invented units

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/borbax-units/Cargo.toml`, `crates/borbax-units/src/lib.rs`
- Test: `crates/borbax-units/src/lib.rs` (`#[cfg(test)] mod tests`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing
- Produces: `Thermal(f64)`, `Quanta(f64)`, `Span(f64)`, `WorldYear(f64)`, `Mass(f64)` — each with `Add`, `Sub`, `Mul<f64>`, `Div<f64>`, `PartialOrd`, `Sum`. `Quanta::ZERO`, `Mass::ZERO`. No cross-unit arithmetic compiles.

**As built — six deviations, all found by executing or reviewing the task.
Later tasks should follow the code, not the text below.**

1. **`Mass` has no `Mul<f64>` and cannot hold a float**, so Step 1's
   `scales_by_scalar` and `sums_over_iterator` tests — written against
   `Mass(2.0)` — cannot compile against Step 3's own implementation. Scaling
   moved to `Span`; `Mass` gained tests for the property it exists for
   (exact conservation under reordering).
2. **The `compile_fail` doctests moved into the crate-level `//!` docs.**
   rustdoc compiles under `cfg(doctest)`, never `cfg(test)`, so Step 5's
   placement inside `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` collects nothing — `cargo test
   --doc` reports `0 tests` and looks like success. Also measured: rustdoc
   does **not** enforce the annotated error code, so each block was verified
   by inversion instead, and each is paired with a block that must compile.
3. **`from_f64_quantised` returns `Result<Self, MassRangeError>`.** A
   `debug_assert!` is compiled out of `--release`, which is the profile that
   mints the goldens; `NaN` then becomes `Mass(0)`, which is conserved
   perfectly forever and breaks no other test. Both reviewers flagged it
   independently. `MAX_MAGNITUDE` is public for the same reason.
4. **`Thermal` has no `Default`.** Zero quanta is the additive identity;
   zero thermals is a physical extreme, and an unset temperature in a config
   struct produces a beaker where nothing happens — silently, because the
   Arrhenius rate clamps before the exponent. `unit!` takes extra derives per
   unit.
5. **§13.1 is enforced by `clippy::disallowed_methods`** (paths in the
   workspace `clippy.toml`), with Step 7's textual scan kept as a
   pre-commit-speed second pass. Step 7's `BANNED_CALLS` matched only the
   method spelling, so `f64::exp(x)`, `<f64>::sin(x)` and `.map(f64::exp)`
   walked past it while xtask printed "all checks passed". The **path
   exemption for `det_math.rs` is gone**: its wrappers call `libm::exp` as a
   free function, so the file needs no exemption from either check.
6. **`--locked` on every cargo invocation** in CI and the pre-push hook. This
   is the task that added the first runtime dependency in a result-affecting
   path, so a stale lock could otherwise let the three legs resolve `libm`
   differently — which the golden matrix would report as a simulation
   portability failure.

**Post-merge review (5 specialists) found nine more, seven fixed on
`fix/task-2-review`. The two headline ones were both in the *fixes* above,
which is the argument for reviewing a fix rather than trusting it.**

- **`impl Add for Mass` claimed a bound that was false by 1024×.** Found
  independently by three reviewers. `MAX_MAGNITUDE` was `i64::MAX / SCALE` in
  *mass units*, so two values the constructor accepted summed to **−2** under
  release wrapping. `MAX_MAGNITUDE` is now derived from a declared
  `MAX_SUMMABLE_TERMS` (2^32) and the whole argument is a `const` assertion
  rather than prose — the old bound no longer compiles.
- **`total_cmp`'s "NaN sorts last" was false on two of the three CI
  platforms.** `f64::total_cmp` orders on the sign bit, and a runtime NaN is
  `0x7ff8…` on aarch64 and `0xfff8…` on x86-64. Its test used `f64::NAN`,
  which is const-evaluated to the positive pattern everywhere — so it passed
  on all three legs while the divergence was live. Renamed `canonical_cmp`,
  which canonicalises both the NaN sign and `-0.0`; the test now builds its
  NaN at runtime, and mutation-testing confirms it fails on x86-64 without
  the fix and passes with it.
- Also fixed: `Mass` kept `Default`, so `unwrap_or_default()` resurrected
  `Mass(0)` past the new `Result` with no lint objecting; `MassRangeError`
  derived `PartialEq` and so was not equal to itself for its headline NaN
  input; `f64::max`/`min` were on neither gate despite being documented
  non-deterministic for `±0.0`; `f32` was invisible to both gates; `cr#"`
  raw strings still blinded the scanner; and the unit operators had no
  `#[inline]`, so they were **not** zero-cost at `opt-level = 1` — measured
  12 instructions against 3, which the dev-profile comment denied.

**The review's forward-looking findings were done here rather than deferred,
wherever the code they touch already exists.** A note in a plan is a bet that
someone reads it at the right moment; a wrapper that cannot be called wrongly
is not.

- **`det_math::acos` exists now, and clamps inside the wrapper.** The renderer
  will want it for axis-angle extraction, where `(tr(R) - 1) / 2` routinely
  exceeds 1 for a numerically-built rotation and unclamped `acos` returns a
  NaN with an architecture-dependent sign. A caller who does not know the
  trace can exceed 3 cannot now get it wrong. Mutation-tested: removing the
  clamp fails `acos_clamps_rather_than_returning_nan`.
- **`canonical_bits()` exists now, on every float unit.** Task 20's state hash
  needs sign-canonicalisation for exactly the reason `canonical_cmp` does, and
  the helper being there first makes `get().to_bits()` the longer thing to
  write.
- **Parallel reductions are banned now**, by `BANNED_PARALLEL_CALLS` in
  `xtask`. rayon is not a dependency, so `disallowed-methods` has no path to
  resolve and a text scan is the only check available — waiting until rayon
  lands means waiting until the guard is retrofitted onto existing call sites.

Two genuinely cannot be done yet, because the code does not exist. They are
recorded **in the tasks that write it**, not here:

| Finding | Lives in |
|---|---|
| `next_normal` must not return non-finite, and the reject/resample/clamp choice changes RNG stream consumption | Task 3 |
| Type the length quantities (`Signature.r`, `ideal_gap`, `SHELL`, `LATTICE_SPAN`) as `Span` | Task 8 — **decide before `Signature` is written** |

**Declined: `overflow-checks` on release.** Measured affordable (+49% on a
12-term `Mol12::mass()`, ~0 on planned paths; the 3-4× rows are bulk
`sum::<Mass>()` over ≥8192 elements, which nothing does). Declined anyway
because it converts a silent wrap into a panic in a workspace that denies
`panic`, and the derived `MAX_MAGNITUDE` above now makes constructor-built
masses unable to overflow at all. Revisit if `from_raw` ever takes untrusted
input.

- [x] **Step 3: Implement the units**

Prepend to `crates/borbax-units/src/lib.rs`:

```rust
//! Invented units (spec §5, G4).
//!
//! Borbax never uses real-world units. Temperature is measured in thermals,
//! energy in quanta, distance in spans, time in world-years, mass in mass
//! units defined by the generated periodic table. These are distinct types
//! and the compiler refuses to mix them — which is the point. It prevents
//! anyone, including us, from quietly reasoning about the simulation as
//! though it modelled something real.

use std::iter::Sum;
use std::ops::{Add, AddAssign, Div, Mul, Neg, Sub};

macro_rules! unit {
    ($name:ident, $doc:literal) => {
        #[doc = $doc]
        // repr(transparent) guarantees `[Quanta; 8]` is layout-compatible
        // with `[f64; 8]`. Without it we would be relying on unspecified
        // repr(Rust) behaviour that merely happens to work today, and the
        // signature kernel depends on that layout to vectorise.
        #[repr(transparent)]
        #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, PartialOrd, Default)]
        pub struct $name(pub f64);

        impl $name {
            pub const ZERO: Self = Self(0.0);

            #[must_use]
            pub fn get(self) -> f64 {
                self.0
            }

            #[must_use]
            pub fn is_finite(self) -> bool {
                self.0.is_finite()
            }
        }

        impl Add for $name {
            type Output = Self;
            fn add(self, rhs: Self) -> Self {
                Self(self.0 + rhs.0)
            }
        }

        impl AddAssign for $name {
            fn add_assign(&mut self, rhs: Self) {
                self.0 += rhs.0;
            }
        }

        impl Sub for $name {
            type Output = Self;
            fn sub(self, rhs: Self) -> Self {
                Self(self.0 - rhs.0)
            }
        }

        impl Neg for $name {
            type Output = Self;
            fn neg(self) -> Self {
                Self(-self.0)
            }
        }

        /// Scaling by a dimensionless scalar is allowed; multiplying two
        /// quantities of the same unit is not, because the result would
        /// have a unit we have no name for.
        impl Mul<f64> for $name {
            type Output = Self;
            fn mul(self, rhs: f64) -> Self {
                Self(self.0 * rhs)
            }
        }

        impl Div<f64> for $name {
            type Output = Self;
            fn div(self, rhs: f64) -> Self {
                Self(self.0 / rhs)
            }
        }

        /// Dividing like by like yields a dimensionless ratio.
        impl Div for $name {
            type Output = f64;
            fn div(self, rhs: Self) -> f64 {
                self.0 / rhs.0
            }
        }

        impl Sum for $name {
            fn sum<I: Iterator<Item = Self>>(iter: I) -> Self {
                iter.fold(Self::ZERO, Add::add)
            }
        }
    };
}

unit!(Thermal, "Temperature, in thermals. Not Kelvin, not Celsius.");
unit!(Quanta, "Energy, in quanta. Not joules.");
unit!(Span, "Distance, in spans. Not metres.");
unit!(WorldYear, "Time, in world-years — defined by the generated world's own orbit.");

/// Mass, in mass units defined by the generated periodic table.
///
/// **Fixed-point, not floating-point** — deliberately the odd one out.
///
/// The global constraints require that a reaction's products have *exactly*
/// the mass of its reactants. Naive `f64` summation over many terms cannot
/// deliver that: you get a tolerance argument instead of a guarantee, and
/// the tolerance has to grow as molecules get larger. Integer arithmetic
/// makes conservation exact by construction, so the test is `==` rather
/// than `(a - b).abs() < eps` — and it stays exact on every platform,
/// which matters for spec §13.4.
///
/// The representation is 1/1024 of a nominal mass unit. Generated atomic
/// masses are quantised onto this grid once, at universe-generation time,
/// so every molecular mass is an exact sum of exact atomic masses forever
/// after.
#[repr(transparent)]
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash, Default)]
pub struct Mass(i64);

impl Mass {
    /// Sub-units per mass unit. A power of two so that quantisation is
    /// exact in binary and the grid has no representation error of its own.
    pub const SCALE: i64 = 1024;
    pub const ZERO: Self = Self(0);

    /// Quantise a real value onto the fixed-point grid.
    ///
    /// Called only at universe-generation time, never in a hot path.
    /// `round_ties_even` is used rather than `round` because it is
    /// symmetric — `round` biases away from zero, which would make
    /// generated mass distributions very slightly heavier than intended.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn from_f64_quantised(v: f64) -> Self {
        Self((v * Self::SCALE as f64).round_ties_even() as i64)
    }

    /// Approximate real value, for display and for rate arithmetic.
    /// Never round-trip through this to perform a conservation check —
    /// compare `Mass` values directly.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn to_f64(self) -> f64 {
        self.0 as f64 / Self::SCALE as f64
    }

    #[must_use]
    pub const fn raw(self) -> i64 {
        self.0
    }

    #[must_use]
    pub const fn from_raw(v: i64) -> Self {
        Self(v)
    }

    /// Scale by a whole number of copies — stoichiometry. Returns `None`
    /// on overflow rather than wrapping, because a silently wrapped mass
    /// would look like a conservation violation somewhere far away.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn checked_mul_count(self, n: u32) -> Option<Self> {
        self.0.checked_mul(i64::from(n)).map(Self)
    }
}

impl Add for Mass {
    type Output = Self;
    fn add(self, rhs: Self) -> Self {
        Self(self.0 + rhs.0)
    }
}

impl AddAssign for Mass {
    fn add_assign(&mut self, rhs: Self) {
        self.0 += rhs.0;
    }
}

impl Sub for Mass {
    type Output = Self;
    fn sub(self, rhs: Self) -> Self {
        Self(self.0 - rhs.0)
    }
}

impl Neg for Mass {
    type Output = Self;
    fn neg(self) -> Self {
        Self(-self.0)
    }
}

impl Sum for Mass {
    fn sum<I: Iterator<Item = Self>>(iter: I) -> Self {
        iter.fold(Self::ZERO, Add::add)
    }
}
```

**Canonical summation shape — decide it here, once.**

`Sum` above folds left, serially. That is a dependency chain of `fadd`s for
the float units and LLVM will not reassociate floats to break it, so it is
not the fastest possible summation. Using multiple accumulators would be
faster and would produce **different bits**.

The decision is: **left fold, serial, everywhere.** It is the obvious
reading, it is what a reviewer expects, and the alternative buys throughput
in a place that is not the bottleneck (§8.6 — the hot loop reads precomputed
per-species records, it does not sum long lists). Retrofitting a different
shape later moves every golden hash in the workspace, which is why it is
being nailed down before the first golden exists rather than after.

Note this concern does not apply to `Mass` at all — integer addition is
associative and exact, so accumulator strategy cannot change its result.
That is a second, quieter argument for the fixed-point choice.

```toml
edition.workspace = true

version = "0.1.0"

[lints]
```

- [x] **Step 6: Add `det_math`, the portable-transcendental chokepoint**

`crates/borbax-units/src/det_math.rs`:

```rust
//! Portable transcendentals (spec §13.1, §13.4).
//!
//! Backed by the `libm` crate — rust-lang's pure-Rust port of MUSL's libm,
//! with no platform dispatch. Apple's libm and glibc genuinely disagree in the
//! last bits of `exp`, `ln`, `sin` and `cos`, and glibc versions disagree with
//! each other. None of them is wrong: IEEE-754 does not specify these to be
//! correctly rounded. It only specifies `+ - * / sqrt`, which is why those stay
//! native.
//!
//! **This lives in `borbax-units` because it must sit below every caller.**
//! `borbax-rng` and `borbax-universe` both need it and both sit below
//! `borbax-molecule`; anywhere higher and this file is unreachable from two of
//! the crates it exists to serve.
//!
//! A direct `.exp()` or `.cos()` on an `f64` anywhere in library code is a
//! determinism bug. There is a CI check for it in `xtask`.

/// `e^x`.
#[must_use]
pub fn exp(x: f64) -> f64 {
    libm::exp(x)
}

/// Natural logarithm. Callers must guarantee `x > 0` — `ln(0)` is `-inf` and
/// will silently poison whatever consumes it (see Task 18's clock).
#[must_use]
pub fn ln(x: f64) -> f64 {
    libm::log(x)
}

#[must_use]
pub fn sin(x: f64) -> f64 {
    libm::sin(x)
}

#[must_use]
pub fn cos(x: f64) -> f64 {
    libm::cos(x)
}

/// `x^y` for runtime `y`. For small integer exponents write the multiplication
/// out instead: `powi`'s multiply tree is chosen by LLVM, so it can be
/// re-associated by a compiler upgrade without our source changing.
#[must_use]
pub fn powf(x: f64, y: f64) -> f64 {
    libm::pow(x, y)
}
```

Add to `crates/borbax-units/Cargo.toml`:

```toml
[dependencies]
libm = { workspace = true }
```

```rust
    #[test]
    fn det_math_agrees_with_the_platform_to_within_a_few_ulp() {
        // Not equality — the whole point is that they may differ. This asserts
        // we have not wired up something wrong, not that they match.
        for x in [-3.0, -0.5, 0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 7.5] {
            assert!((det_math::exp(x) - x.exp()).abs() < 1e-12 * x.exp().max(1.0));
        }
        for x in [0.25, 1.0, 2.0, 1000.0] {
            assert!((det_math::ln(x) - x.ln()).abs() < 1e-12);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn det_math_is_deterministic_within_a_process() {
        assert_eq!(det_math::exp(1.0).to_bits(), det_math::exp(1.0).to_bits());
    }
```

- [x] **Step 7: Add the xtask check that nothing bypasses it**

Extend `xtask` with a scan for direct transcendental calls in library code.
This is the check that stops the chokepoint being quietly bypassed six months
from now, and it is cheap:

```rust
/// §13.1 — every transcendental must route through `borbax_units::det_math`.
/// A direct call is invisible locally and only diverges on another platform,
/// so a grep at commit time is worth more than a code review.
const BANNED_CALLS: &[&str] =
    &[".exp()", ".ln()", ".log(", ".sin()", ".cos()", ".tan()", ".powf(", ".powi("];

/// The one file allowed to call the platform, identified by its *path*.
///
/// Matching on the filename alone would exempt a `det_math.rs` anywhere in the
/// tree, which is the precise bypass this check exists to prevent: a second
/// copy in whichever crate happened to need one is how the chokepoint stops
/// being a chokepoint. There is exactly one, it is in `borbax-units` because
/// two crates below `borbax-molecule` call it (spec §18.3), and moving it is a
/// deliberate change to this constant rather than a silently-tolerated
/// duplicate.
const DET_MATH_PATH: &str = "crates/borbax-units/src/det_math.rs";

fn check_no_platform_transcendentals(root: &Path, failures: &mut Vec<String>) -> Result<(), String> {
    let crates_dir = root.join("crates");
    if !crates_dir.exists() {
        return Ok(());
    }
    // Scanned wholesale rather than from a list of crate names. A hand-kept
    // list silently exempts every crate added after it was written, which is
    // the same failure mode as the filename exemption above.
    for entry in walk(&crates_dir)? {
        if entry.extension().and_then(|e| e.to_str()) != Some("rs") {
            continue;
        }
        // Component-wise comparison against the workspace-relative path.
        // `Path` treats `/` as a separator on Windows too, so the literal
        // above is portable.
        let rel = entry.strip_prefix(root).unwrap_or(&entry);
        if rel == Path::new(DET_MATH_PATH) {
            continue;
        }
        let src = std::fs::read_to_string(&entry).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        for (i, line) in src.lines().enumerate() {
            // Test modules may use anything.
            if line.trim_start().starts_with("//") {
                continue;
            }
            for call in BANNED_CALLS {
                if line.contains(call) {
                    failures.push(format!(
                        "§13.1: platform transcendental {call} at {}:{} — route through det_math",
                        entry.display(),
                        i + 1
                    ));
                }
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
```

The implementer will need to exclude `#[cfg(test)]` regions; a simple
brace-depth tracker from the `mod tests` line is sufficient and does not need
to be clever. Wire it into `main` alongside the other two checks, and — per
Task 1 Step 5 — plant a direct `.exp()` once and watch it fail before trusting
it.

### Task 3: borbax-rng — counter-based deterministic streams

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/borbax-rng/Cargo.toml`, `crates/borbax-rng/src/lib.rs`
- Test: `crates/borbax-rng/src/lib.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing
- Produces: `Domain` enum, `Stream::new(seed: u64, domain: Domain, index: u64) -> Stream`, `Stream::next_u64(&mut self) -> u64`, `Stream::next_f64(&mut self) -> f64` in `[0,1)`, `Stream::next_range(&mut self, n: u64) -> u64` in `[0,n)`, `Stream::sub(seed: u64, domain: Domain, index: u64, sub: u64) -> Stream`

**From the Task 2 review — settle this while writing `next_normal`, not after.**
`Mass::from_f64_quantised` is fallible, and the only realistic way Task 4 feeds
it a bad value is a non-finite draw: `mass = base + next_normal() * spread`,
and Box-Muller goes through `ln`, whose own doc warns `ln(0)` is `-inf`.

Make `next_normal` incapable of returning a non-finite value, so that `Err` arm
is unreachable in the generator and the decision is taken once here rather than
N times downstream. **The choice is not free of consequences**: rejecting and
redrawing consumes further RNG draws and shifts every subsequent value in the
`Domain` stream — changing the entire generated universe — where clamping `u1`
away from zero does not. Whichever is chosen, pin the stream consumption in a
test, because it is part of the determinism contract from that moment.

**Every step below is ticked, and the bodies describe a design that was
replaced during execution.** Three reviewers and CodeRabbit each flagged a
different stale detail here — a `fork` call that no longer exists, a "four
actual `u64` values" that is now twelve, a "7 passed" that is now 39 — and each
time the question was whether to edit a completed record. Settling it once:

**The step bodies are a historical record and are not maintained. The crate is
the record of what shipped.** Where a body was edited before this rule was
written it has been left, rather than reverted to a state matching neither.
What actually shipped is in `crates/borbax-rng/`, and the deviations are listed
under "As built" at the end of this task. Do not read a ticked step as a
description of current code.

- [x] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`crates/borbax-rng/src/lib.rs`:

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn same_inputs_give_same_sequence() {
        let mut a = Stream::new(42, Domain::Universe, 0);
        let mut b = Stream::new(42, Domain::Universe, 0);
        let sa: Vec<u64> = (0..64).map(|_| a.next_u64()).collect();
        let sb: Vec<u64> = (0..64).map(|_| b.next_u64()).collect();
        assert_eq!(sa, sb);
    }

    #[test]
    fn different_domains_are_independent() {
        let mut a = Stream::new(42, Domain::Universe, 0);
        let mut b = Stream::new(42, Domain::Fold, 0);
        let sa: Vec<u64> = (0..64).map(|_| a.next_u64()).collect();
        let sb: Vec<u64> = (0..64).map(|_| b.next_u64()).collect();
        assert_ne!(sa, sb);
    }

    #[test]
    fn different_indices_are_independent() {
        let mut a = Stream::new(42, Domain::Fold, 0);
        let mut b = Stream::new(42, Domain::Fold, 1);
        assert_ne!(a.next_u64(), b.next_u64());
    }

    #[test]
    fn floats_are_in_unit_interval() {
        let mut s = Stream::new(7, Domain::Reaction, 0);
        for _ in 0..10_000 {
            let v = s.next_f64();
            assert!((0.0..1.0).contains(&v), "out of range: {v}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn range_is_bounded_and_covers() {
        let mut s = Stream::new(7, Domain::Reaction, 0);
        let mut seen = [false; 6];
        for _ in 0..10_000 {
            let v = s.next_range(6);
            assert!(v < 6);
            seen[v as usize] = true;
        }
        assert!(seen.iter().all(|&x| x), "some values never produced");
    }

    /// The mean of many uniform draws should sit near 0.5. This is a smoke
    /// test for gross bias, not a serious statistical test — a mixer that
    /// passed this and nothing else would still be inadequate, but one that
    /// fails it is definitely broken.
    #[test]
    fn is_not_grossly_biased() {
        let mut s = Stream::new(99, Domain::Decay, 0);
        let n = 100_000;
        let mean: f64 = (0..n).map(|_| s.next_f64()).sum::<f64>() / f64::from(n);
        assert!((mean - 0.5).abs() < 0.01, "mean was {mean}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn forking_is_deterministic_and_independent() {
        let base = Stream::new(1, Domain::Fold, 0);
        let mut f1 = Stream::sub(1, Domain::Fold, 0, 3);
        let mut f2 = Stream::sub(1, Domain::Fold, 0, 3);
        let mut f3 = Stream::sub(1, Domain::Fold, 0, 4);
        assert_eq!(f1.next_u64(), f2.next_u64());
        assert_ne!(f1.next_u64(), f3.next_u64());
    }

    /// Golden values. If the mixer changes, every golden run hash in the
    /// project changes with it — so this is pinned deliberately and a
    /// failure here means "you just changed all the physics", not
    /// "the test is stale".
    #[test]
    fn golden_sequence_is_pinned() {
        let mut s = Stream::new(0, Domain::Universe, 0);
        let got: Vec<u64> = (0..4).map(|_| s.next_u64()).collect();
        assert_eq!(got.len(), 4);
        // Fill these in from the first run (Step 5), then never change them
        // without regenerating every golden in the workspace.
        assert_eq!(got, GOLDEN_UNIVERSE_0);
    }

    const GOLDEN_UNIVERSE_0: [u64; 4] = [0, 0, 0, 0];
}
```

- [x] **Step 2: Run tests to verify they fail**

Run: `cargo test -p borbax-rng`
Expected: FAIL — `cannot find type Stream in this scope`

- [x] **Step 3: Implement the stream**

Prepend to `crates/borbax-rng/src/lib.rs`:

```rust
//! Counter-based deterministic randomness (spec §13.1).
//!
//! Every random draw in Borbax comes from here. Streams are *counter-based*
//! rather than stateful-shared: a stream's output is a pure function of
//! (seed, domain, index, counter), so two threads drawing from different
//! streams cannot influence each other and results never depend on
//! scheduling order. That property is what makes bit-for-bit reproducibility
//! survive parallelism.
//!
//! Never use `rand`, thread-local RNGs, or `HashMap`'s default hasher in
//! any code path that affects simulation results.

/// Which subsystem a stream belongs to. Streams in different domains are
/// independent, so adding a new random draw in one subsystem cannot shift
/// the sequence any other subsystem sees — which means a change to folding
/// does not silently perturb reaction outcomes.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
#[repr(u64)]
pub enum Domain {
    Universe = 1,
    Naming = 2,
    Molecule = 3,
    Fold = 4,
    Reaction = 5,
    Decay = 6,
    Beaker = 7,
    Shadow = 8,
    /// Hashing only — cache keys and content digests. Separated from the live
    /// domains so that using a `Stream` as a hash function cannot couple to
    /// the subsystem it names: adding a draw in folding must not change fold
    /// cache keys.
    Hash = 9,
}
```

**Discriminants are explicit and permanent, and a new variant may only be
appended.** This is the seam that lets physics be extended later without
invalidating every world already generated, and it costs nothing to establish
now.

`Stream::new` derives its key from `(seed, domain, index)`, so streams are
independent by construction: a draw taken from `Domain::Fold` cannot shift a
draw from `Domain::Universe`. The `Hash` variant above already relies on this
for a smaller reason. Generalised, it means **a V3 law whose constants come
from a new `Domain = 10` leaves every constant an existing universe already
generated bit-identical** — the periodic table, the bond matrix, the binding
weights, all unchanged.

Without that discipline the alternative is grim: adding a single
`rng.next_f64()` to universe generation shifts every subsequent draw in that
stream, so every existing seed silently generates a *different* periodic table.
Worlds shared as `U-7F3A21C9` would stop meaning what they meant (§6), and
nothing would announce it.

Two rules follow, and both are cheap:

1. **Append; never renumber, never reuse.** A removed subsystem's discriminant
   is retired, not recycled. Reordering the enum is a physics change.
2. **New physics draws from a new `Domain`.** Reaching into an existing one to
   add "just one more constant" is the mistake this enum exists to prevent.

- [x] **Step 1b: Pin every domain's stream against future additions**

The invariant above is worth exactly as much as its test. Without one, the
failure is silent and arrives years later.

```rust
    /// Adding a `Domain` variant must not perturb any existing stream.
    ///
    /// These are golden values, and the point is that they can only change
    /// deliberately. If appending `Domain::Field = 10` moves any of them, the
    /// enum's discriminants have been renumbered or `Stream::new`'s mixing has
    /// changed — either of which regenerates the chemistry of every universe
    /// ever produced. A reviewer seeing this test fail should treat it exactly
    /// like a moved golden hash.
    #[test]
    fn every_domain_stream_is_pinned() {
        // (domain, first three u64 draws from seed 0x5EED, index 0)
        const PINNED: &[(Domain, [u64; 3])] = &[ /* filled in at Step 5 */ ];
        for (domain, want) in PINNED {
            let mut s = Stream::new(0x5EED, *domain, 0);
            let got = [s.next_u64(), s.next_u64(), s.next_u64()];
            assert_eq!(&got, want, "{domain:?} stream moved");
        }
    }

    /// The property the pinning test protects, stated directly: a stream is a
    /// pure function of (seed, domain, index), so no domain can influence
    /// another regardless of how many draws it takes.
    #[test]
    fn domains_are_independent_of_each_others_draw_counts() {
        let mut fold = Stream::new(0x5EED, Domain::Fold, 0);
        for _ in 0..1000 {
            let _ = fold.next_u64();
        }
        let mut a = Stream::new(0x5EED, Domain::Universe, 0);
        let mut b = Stream::new(0x5EED, Domain::Universe, 0);
        assert_eq!(a.next_u64(), b.next_u64());
    }
```

Fill `PINNED` at Step 5 alongside the other golden values, from the same run.

**The rest of Step 3 is not reproduced here, and that is deliberate.**

An earlier version of this task carried a second copy of the implementation —
`SplitMix64`'s finalizer, a `fork` built on key re-derivation, and a
`next_normal` clamping to `f64::MIN_POSITIVE`. **Every one of those was
replaced during execution**, the last after review measured that the
`MIN_POSITIVE` clamp put an atom of probability at 37.640σ where the reachable
support ends at 8.5717σ. Task 3's boxes are ticked, so a stale copy here would
read as a *verified* record of what shipped.

The generator is **Philox 4x64-10**, transcribed from Random123 with the three
published known-answer vectors asserted in-tree. Read
`crates/borbax-rng/src/philox.rs` and `crates/borbax-rng/src/lib.rs`; they are
the record. Two doc comments there are worth reading before writing any code
that draws: `Stream::sub`'s paragraph on why a per-molecule index is
per-molecule state in disguise (§8.6, §9.5), and `Domain::Shadow`'s on why its
own original justification was vacuous.

**`fork` became `sub`, and the shape is the point.** A `fork(&self, i) -> Stream`
is an operation on a stream returning a stream, so `base.fork(a).fork(b)`
type-checks — and was measured bit-identical to `base.fork(b)`, because the
child overwrote its parent's coordinate rather than extending it. As a
constructor there is no such operation, so the second level cannot be written
down: `E0599`, verified against a consumer crate. Bit-identical to the old
`fork` of a root, so no golden moved.
