```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::graph::MAX_ATOMS;
    use borbax_rng::{Domain, Stream};
    use borbax_universe::ElementId;

    /// Relabel a molecule by a permutation. An isomorphic graph must reduce
    /// to the same canonical form — that is the whole contract.
    fn permute(m: &Mol12, p: &[u8]) -> Mol12 {
        let mut out = Mol12::new();
        for i in 0..m.n {
            out.add_atom(ElementId(m.elem[p[i as usize] as usize]));
        }
        for i in 0..m.n {
            for j in (i + 1)..m.n {
                let o = m.bond_order(p[i as usize], p[j as usize]);
                if o > 0 {
                    out.add_bond(i, j, o);
                }
            }
        }
        out
    }

    fn random_molecule(rng: &mut Stream, n_elements: u8) -> Mol12 {
        let mut m = Mol12::new();
        let n = 2 + rng.next_range(MAX_ATOMS as u64 - 1) as u8;
        for _ in 0..n {
            m.add_atom(ElementId(rng.next_range(u64::from(n_elements)) as u8));
        }
        // Spanning tree first, so the molecule is always connected.
        for i in 1..n {
            let parent = rng.next_range(u64::from(i)) as u8;
            // **Draw from `BondOrder::ALL`, not `1 + next_range(3)`.** The
            // literal 3 is the same stale constant `N_ORDERS` just stopped
            // being: with it, planes 3, 4 and 5 are never set anywhere in the
            // suite, so `encode`'s new plane writes, `refine`'s new per-plane
            // popcounts and `CanonForm`'s derived `Ord` over the new planes all
            // go untested while both 2000-iteration property tests report green.
            // A `proptest` `prop::sample::select(BondOrder::ALL)` strategy tracks
            // the array rather than a literal and makes this unrepresentable;
            // `proptest` is a dev-dependency, so the bar is low.
            let o = BondOrder::ALL[rng.next_range(BondOrder::ALL.len() as u64) as usize];
            m.add_bond(parent, i, o)?;
        }
        // A few extra bonds, respecting a valence cap so we never build
        // graphs that are chemically impossible (see the search-bound note).
        for _ in 0..rng.next_range(4) {
            let a = rng.next_range(u64::from(n)) as u8;
            let b = rng.next_range(u64::from(n)) as u8;
            if a != b && m.valence_used(a) < 6 && m.valence_used(b) < 6 {
                m.add_bond(a, b, 1);
            }
        }
        m
    }

    /// Element ids must span the real range, not `0..6`. The colouring bug
    /// this catches is invisible with a handful of low ids and produces
    /// out-of-bounds writes — or, in release, a masked shift and an unstable
    /// colouring — with realistic ones.
    #[test]
    fn relabelling_is_stable_across_the_full_element_range() {
        let mut rng = Stream::new(17, Domain::Molecule, 0);
        for _ in 0..2_000 {
            let m = random_molecule(&mut rng, 120);
            let (want, stats) = canonicalise(&m);
            assert!(!stats.cap_hit);
            let mut p: Vec<u8> = (0..m.n).collect();
            for i in (1..p.len()).rev() {
                p.swap(i, rng.next_range((i + 1) as u64) as usize);
            }
            assert_eq!(canonicalise(&permute(&m, &p)).0, want);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn relabelling_does_not_change_the_canonical_form() {
        let mut rng = Stream::new(1, Domain::Molecule, 0);
        for _ in 0..2_000 {
            let m = random_molecule(&mut rng, 6);
            let want = canonicalise(&m).0;
            // Try several relabellings of the same molecule.
            for _ in 0..4 {
                let mut p: Vec<u8> = (0..m.n).collect();
                for i in (1..p.len()).rev() {
                    p.swap(i, rng.next_range((i + 1) as u64) as usize);
                }
                assert_eq!(canonicalise(&permute(&m, &p)).0, want, "relabelling changed the form");
            }
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn different_molecules_get_different_forms() {
        let mut a = Mol12::new();
        a.add_atom(ElementId(0));
        a.add_atom(ElementId(1));
        a.add_bond(0, 1, 1);

        let mut b = Mol12::new();
        b.add_atom(ElementId(0));
        b.add_atom(ElementId(1));
        b.add_bond(0, 1, 2); // same atoms, different bond order

        assert_ne!(canonicalise(&a).0, canonicalise(&b).0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn element_labels_distinguish_otherwise_identical_graphs() {
        let mut a = Mol12::new();
        a.add_atom(ElementId(0));
        a.add_atom(ElementId(0));
        a.add_bond(0, 1, 1);

        let mut b = Mol12::new();
        b.add_atom(ElementId(0));
        b.add_atom(ElementId(2));
        b.add_bond(0, 1, 1);

        assert_ne!(canonicalise(&a).0, canonicalise(&b).0);
    }

    /// The perf review predicted refinement alone discretises for the large
    /// majority of small labelled molecules, leaving the expensive search a
    /// rarely-taken path. Measure it rather than believe it — the number
    /// decides how much this matters later.
    #[test]
    fn search_rarely_needs_to_branch() {
        let mut rng = Stream::new(2, Domain::Molecule, 0);
        let (mut total, mut branched, mut worst) = (0, 0, 0);
        for _ in 0..5_000 {
            let m = random_molecule(&mut rng, 6);
            let (_, stats) = canonicalise(&m);
            total += 1;
            if stats.leaves > 1 {
                branched += 1;
            }
            worst = worst.max(stats.leaves);
        }
        let pct = 100.0 * f64::from(branched) / f64::from(total);
        println!("branched on {pct:.1}% of molecules, worst case {worst} leaves");
        assert!(worst < SEARCH_LEAF_CAP, "hit the search cap — investigate, do not raise it");
    }
}
```

```rust
//! Canonical labelling, so isomorphic molecules intern to the same species.
//!
//! Colour refinement (1-WL) followed by individualization-refinement, taking
//! the lexicographically smallest encoding. At twelve atoms with element and
//! bond-order labels, refinement alone discretises the colouring for the
//! large majority of molecules, so the search is a rarely-taken path — see
//! `search_rarely_needs_to_branch`, which measures this rather than assuming.

use crate::graph::{Mol12, MAX_ATOMS, N_ORDERS};

/// Upper-triangle pair count for 12 vertices: 12 * 11 / 2 = 66, which fits
/// one `u128` per bond order with room to spare.
///
/// **The "three planes vs a packed 2-bit encoding" argument here is stale
/// twice.** There are `BondOrder::PLANES` planes now, not three, and the packed
/// form would need 3 bits (198), not 2 (132) — so "132 bits does not fit" is
/// arithmetic about a layout nobody is proposing. What is true: `CanonForm` is
/// the species intern key and its `planes` array went 48 -> 96 bytes when the
/// cap rose, and `refine`'s per-vertex `sig` rows went 444 -> 876 bytes of stack
/// per round. Measured over 2000 universes, order 6 is formable for **0.58%** of
/// bondable ordered pairs and reachable at all in **23.2%** of universes, and
/// order >= 5 is 5.13% of pairs — so the top planes are all-zero almost always.
/// Whether a packed representation earns its complexity is
/// `rust-performance-expert`'s call and is **not** decided here; if they take
/// it, the coupling to `MAX` disappears and this finding class with it.
const N_PAIRS: usize = MAX_ATOMS * (MAX_ATOMS - 1) / 2;

/// Ceiling on leaves explored by the individualization search.
///
/// **THIS SAMPLE'S JUSTIFICATION IS FALSE — see the shipped constant.** It said
/// valence bounds the automorphism group so a cap hit means an impossible
/// molecule. Degree does not bound `|Aut|`: twelve isolated atoms give 12! at
/// degree 0, and K(6,6) over a valence-6 element is connected, valence-legal,
/// accepted without one refusal, and needs 1 036 800 leaves. The cap bounds
/// search cost. Kept here only so this block matches what was written; read
/// `canonical.rs` for the text that is true.
pub const SEARCH_LEAF_CAP: u32 = 50_000;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SearchStats {
    pub leaves: u32,
    pub refinement_rounds: u32,
    /// Set when the search hit `SEARCH_LEAF_CAP` and returned a **partial**
    /// minimum. That is not a slow answer, it is a wrong one: the same
    /// molecule under a different labelling can explore a different prefix and
    /// produce a different canonical form, so it interns as a second species.
    /// Sticky so callers cannot miss it.
    pub cap_hit: bool,
}

/// A molecule's canonical encoding. Comparison is a few integer compares,
/// which is what makes this usable as an intern key.
///
/// Field order defines the derived `Ord`, and therefore defines which
/// labelling counts as "smallest". It is part of the physics: changing it
/// changes every species id and every golden hash.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub struct CanonForm {
    pub n: u8,
    pub elem: [ElementId; MAX_ATOMS],
    pub planes: [u128; N_ORDERS],
}

#[inline]
fn pair_bit(i: usize, j: usize) -> u32 {
    // Canonical index for the unordered pair {i, j} with i < j.
    let (lo, hi) = if i < j { (i, j) } else { (j, i) };
    (hi * (hi - 1) / 2 + lo) as u32
}

/// Encode a molecule under a given vertex ordering.
/// `order[k]` is the original vertex placed at canonical position `k`.
fn encode(m: &Mol12, order: &[u8]) -> CanonForm {
    let mut elem = [0u8; MAX_ATOMS];
    let mut planes = [0u128; N_ORDERS];
    for (k, &v) in order.iter().enumerate() {
        elem[k] = m.elem[v as usize];
    }
    for a in 0..order.len() {
        for b in (a + 1)..order.len() {
            let o = m.bond_order(order[a], order[b]);
            if o > 0 {
                planes[(o - 1) as usize] |= 1u128 << pair_bit(a, b);
            }
        }
    }
    CanonForm { n: m.n, elem, planes }
}

/// One round of colour refinement. Returns the number of colour classes.
///
/// A vertex's signature under the current colouring is its own colour plus,
/// for every (bond order, colour) pair, how many neighbours it has of that
/// colour — all computed as popcounts over colour masks. No allocation, no
/// hashing, and no map iteration whose order could vary.
fn refine(m: &Mol12, colour: &mut [u8; MAX_ATOMS], stats: &mut SearchStats) -> usize {
    let n = m.n as usize;
    if n == 0 {
        return 0;
    }
    loop {
        stats.refinement_rounds += 1;
        let n_colours = usize::from(*colour[..n].iter().max().unwrap_or(&0)) + 1;

        let mut masks = [0u16; MAX_ATOMS];
        for v in 0..n {
            masks[colour[v] as usize] |= 1 << v;
        }

        // Signature per vertex: own colour, then counts per (order, colour).
        let mut sig = [[0u8; 1 + N_ORDERS * MAX_ATOMS]; MAX_ATOMS];
        for v in 0..n {
            sig[v][0] = colour[v];
            let mut k = 1;
            for plane in &m.adj {
                for c in 0..n_colours {
                    sig[v][k] = (plane[v] & masks[c]).count_ones() as u8;
                    k += 1;
                }
            }
        }

        // Rank signatures to produce new colours. Insertion-order-free: the
        // rank of a signature is how many distinct signatures are strictly
        // smaller, which depends only on the multiset of signatures.
        let mut new_colour = [0u8; MAX_ATOMS];
        for v in 0..n {
            let mut rank = 0u8;
            for w in 0..n {
                if sig[w] < sig[v] {
                    // Count distinct smaller signatures only.
                    if !(0..w).any(|x| sig[x] == sig[w]) {
                        rank += 1;
                    }
                }
            }
            new_colour[v] = rank;
        }

        let before = count_classes(colour, n);
        let after = count_classes(&new_colour, n);
        *colour = new_colour;
        if after == before {
            return after;
        }
    }
}

fn count_classes(colour: &[u8; MAX_ATOMS], n: usize) -> usize {
    let mut seen = 0u16;
    for v in 0..n {
        seen |= 1 << colour[v];
    }
    seen.count_ones() as usize
}

/// Reduce a molecule to its canonical form.
///
/// Returns the form plus search statistics, which exist so the branch rate
/// can be measured rather than assumed.
#[must_use]
pub fn canonicalise(m: &Mol12) -> (CanonForm, SearchStats) {
    let n = m.n as usize;
    let mut stats = SearchStats::default();
    if n == 0 {
        return (CanonForm { n: 0, elem: [0; MAX_ATOMS], planes: [0; N_ORDERS] }, stats);
    }

    // Initial colouring by element, **densified**.
    //
    // Assigning `colour[v] = m.elem[v]` directly is wrong and fails silently.
    // Element ids run to 119, while `refine`'s `masks` is `[u16; MAX_ATOMS]`,
    // its `sig` rows are sized for at most `MAX_ATOMS` colours, and
    // `count_classes` computes `1u16 << colour[v]` — which panics in debug and
    // **masks the shift** in release, so refinement stops before it is stable
    // and two relabellings of the same molecule intern as different species.
    //
    // Every test in this task uses `ElementId(0..5)`, so all of them pass.
    // Ranking the ids makes every colour dense in `0..n`, which is what the
    // three arrays are actually sized for.
    let mut colour = [0u8; MAX_ATOMS];
    for v in 0..n {
        // Rank of this element among the distinct elements present. Depends
        // only on the multiset of element ids, so it stays isomorphism-invariant.
        let mut rank = 0u8;
        for w in 0..n {
            if m.elem[w] < m.elem[v] && !(0..w).any(|x| m.elem[x] == m.elem[w]) {
                rank += 1;
            }
        }
        colour[v] = rank;
    }
    debug_assert!(colour[..n].iter().all(|&c| (c as usize) < MAX_ATOMS));
    let mut best: Option<CanonForm> = None;
    search(m, colour, &mut best, &mut stats);
    // `search` always reaches at least one leaf for n > 0.
    let form = best.unwrap_or_else(|| encode(m, &(0..m.n).collect::<Vec<u8>>()));
    (form, stats)
}

fn search(m: &Mol12, mut colour: [u8; MAX_ATOMS], best: &mut Option<CanonForm>, stats: &mut SearchStats) {
    let n = m.n as usize;
    if stats.leaves >= SEARCH_LEAF_CAP {
        // Returning here yields a partial minimum, which is a *wrong* canonical
        // form rather than a slow one — see `SearchStats::cap_hit`.
        stats.cap_hit = true;
        debug_assert!(false, "canonicalisation hit the leaf cap: the molecule is not valence-legal, or the cap is wrong");
        return;
    }
    let classes = refine(m, &mut colour, stats);

    if classes == n {
        // Discrete: the colouring *is* an ordering.
        stats.leaves += 1;
        let mut order = vec![0u8; n];
        for v in 0..n {
            order[colour[v] as usize] = v as u8;
        }
        let form = encode(m, &order);
        if best.as_ref().is_none_or(|b| form < *b) {
            *best = Some(form);
        }
        return;
    }

    // Target cell: the smallest colour class with more than one member.
    // Deterministic choice — "smallest colour, then lowest index" — so the
    // search tree is identical on every platform.
    let mut target = u8::MAX;
    for c in 0..=u8::MAX {
        let members: Vec<usize> = (0..n).filter(|&v| colour[v] == c).collect();
        if members.len() > 1 {
            target = c;
            break;
        }
        if c == u8::MAX {
            break;
        }
    }
    if target == u8::MAX {
        return;
    }

    for v in 0..n {
        if colour[v] != target {
            continue;
        }
        // Individualize v: it keeps the target colour, everyone else in the
        // cell (and every higher colour) shifts up by one.
        let mut c2 = colour;
        for w in 0..n {
            if c2[w] > target || (c2[w] == target && w != v) {
                c2[w] += 1;
            }
        }
        search(m, c2, best, stats);
    }
}
```

```bash

git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'
feat(molecule): atom graphs and canonical labelling

Mol12 stores adjacency as bitset rows, which keeps a whole molecule in 72
bytes and turns colour refinement into popcounts over colour masks — no
allocation, no hashing, and no map iteration whose order could vary
(spec §13.1).

Canonicalisation is 1-WL refinement plus individualization-refinement,
taking the lexicographically smallest encoding. CanonForm's field order
defines which labelling counts as smallest, so it is physics: changing it
changes every species id and every golden hash.

The search leaf cap is deliberately not a tuning knob. Valence bounds
degree in any chemically reachable molecule, so hitting the cap means
something impossible was constructed — investigate the construction
rather than raising the cap. If it is ever hit legitimately, bind to
nauty (§18.2) instead of growing the search.

Branch rate measured rather than assumed: <FILL IN from test output>

MrReasonable <4990954+MrReasonable@users.noreply.github.com>
```

### Task 7: Geodesic directions and the rotation permutation table

**This task was executed as a lift, not as a fresh implementation, and the
Step 3 code this section used to contain has been deleted rather than ticked.**
Read the next three paragraphs before concluding the plan was skipped.

`geodesic.rs` was written during the G2 locality experiment, months of plan-time
early, because G2 could not measure signature locality without a rotation table.
It lived in `borbax-experiments` with a module doc saying it was Task 7's
content and would "lift into `borbax-molecule` unchanged". By the time Task 7
came due that file had been through the G2 review rounds and was **ahead of the
code written here**: 16 tests against this section's 11, `thiserror` on
`GeoError`, an `exact_index` helper, and four checks that did not exist in the
plan at all — `rotation_matrices` as real geometry, the test tying the integer
table back to it, a per-element determinant check, and `minus_identity_is_not_
in_the_group`, which is the specific hazard CLAUDE.md names.

Executing the plan literally would therefore have **regressed** the module. The
plan's Step 3 was the ancestor of the shipped code, not an alternative to it.

**The lift was not "unchanged", and that is the transferable part.** **Four**
comments asserted "this lifts into `borbax-molecule`" as a future event — six
matching lines, since the `total_cmp` block carries three of them — and each
had to become a statement about a file that has arrived. One of them made four
measured claims and instructed its reader to re-measure them on arrival, which
is how the **third**, "antipodes bit-exact", was found to be **false**,
benignly, at every zero component. (The fourth, the norm bound, is true and
retained.) It had never been tested either way. See "What the lift changed"
below.

**And the argument this section originally made for the lift was the weak
one.** It argued from test counts. The compiler settles it: substituting the
plan's own Step 3 code for `geodesic.rs` and running the gate's clippy leg
gives **71 errors** — 38 `indexing_slicing`, 9 `as_conversions` (two of them
genuine `usize → u8` truncation), 9 `unwrap_used`, **3 `disallowed_methods` on
`f64::total_cmp` (§13.4) and 2 on `f64::min` (§13.1)**, plus
`needless_range_loop`, `manual_midpoint` and four `missing_docs`. The plan's
Step 3 fails leg 2 of the six-leg gate in 71 places, five of them determinism
violations, into a crate that currently has zero. That is a verdict, not a
judgement call.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/borbax-molecule/src/geodesic.rs` (from `experiments/src/geodesic.rs`)
- Delete: `experiments/src/geodesic.rs` — there must be exactly one copy. If the
  table G2 measured ever diverged from the table the binding kernel searches,
  the measurement would be invalidated with nothing failing.
- Edit: `experiments/Cargo.toml` (depend on `borbax-molecule`),
  `experiments/src/lib.rs` (`pub use borbax_molecule::geodesic;`),
  `crates/borbax-molecule/src/lib.rs` (declare and re-export)
- Test: same file

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing
- Produces: `N_ROTATIONS`, `GeoError`, `Geodesic<D>`, `Geodesic::build`,
  `Geodesic::{dirs, perms, anti}`, `vertex_count`, `Mat3`, `apply_mat`,
  `is_identity`, `rotation_matrices`

`borbax-experiments` is a leaf binary crate, so depending *upward* on
`borbax-molecule` introduces no cycle and does not violate the crate order.

#### What the lift changed

Every item here is a difference between the file in `borbax-experiments` and
the file now in `borbax-molecule`. Nothing else moved.

1. **The module doc** no longer says it will lift; it says it has, and why
   `borbax-experiments` re-exports instead of copying.
2. **`icosahedron`'s "bit-exact negation" claim — the third of the four, not
   the fourth — was wrong** and is corrected.
   Antipodes are exact *numerically* — `dirs[anti[i]][k] + dirs[i][k] == 0.0`
   at every index at all three levels — but not bitwise: the zero component of
   each `(0, ±1, ±φ)` vertex is stored `+0.0` and negates to `-0.0`. Measured:
   12, 24 and 48 bit mismatches at D = 12, 42, 162, exactly the zero-component
   counts, and **zero** numeric mismatches. The numeric equality is what
   `dist2` and `build_anti`'s tolerance see, so the tolerance was always safe;
   the comment was describing it wrongly.
3. **`build_anti`'s doc** now states the tolerance has 24 orders of headroom
   over a measured `dist2` of exactly `0.0`, and names the ±0.0 subtlety.
4. **The `total_cmp` exemption comment** kept its two load-bearing claims (no
   `-0.0`, no NaN in `dirs`) and dropped the false one. Its instruction to
   re-measure on lift was followed rather than copied.
5. **`min_pair_dist2`'s** justification no longer rests on "this function lifts
   into `borbax-molecule`"; it rests on squared distances not being negative.
6. **`is_identity`'s doc** said "Task 8's binding kernel needs the same
   predicate". The binding kernel is **Task 10**. Its reference to
   `g2::ROTATION_MUST_MOVE_RAW` also became prose, that symbol now living one
   crate up.
7. **The test module dropped `.unwrap()`** for this crate's
   `unwrap_or_else(|| unreachable!(…))` idiom. `clippy.toml` sets neither
   `allow-unwrap-in-tests` nor `allow-expect-in-tests`, so the workspace deny
   reaches inside `#[cfg(test)]`, and CLAUDE.md records `borbax-molecule` as
   carrying zero bare unwraps — a property the verbatim lift would have broken.
8. **Two tests were added**, both for gaps the lift itself created or exposed:
   - `the_direction_set_carries_no_negative_zero_and_no_nan`, at all three
     levels. This is the premise the `#[expect(clippy::disallowed_methods)]` on
     the `total_cmp` sort rests on, and it was previously only a sentence.
   - `is_identity_selects_exactly_one_of_the_sixty`. `is_identity`'s only
     coverage was a call site in the G2 harness, which does **not** come with
     the module — so without this the lift would have shipped it untested into
     a crate whose kernel will depend on it.
9. **Three tests were widened from D = 42 to all three levels**:
   `directions_are_unit_vectors`, the antipode involution, and the
   permutation-table/geometry agreement. The last matters most: it is the only
   test tying the integer table to real rotations, and confining the one
   geometric check to a single `D` is how a level-dependent indexing error
   survives. The inverse mistake is already recorded in this project — a
   coefficient fitted at D = 162, the one level its table never used.
10. **The antipode assertion was tightened from `< 1e-12` to exact equality**,
    and `f64::max` in a new test was replaced with an explicit comparison
    (§13.1 bans it for the reason `min_pair_dist2` documents; `clippy` caught
    it, not review).

#### What the six-lane review then changed

Five specialists plus a CodeRabbit CLI pass. Corroborated findings first.

11. **`the_canonical_direction_ordering_is_pinned`** — the review's most
    important result, found independently by the determinism and geometry lanes
    from different instruments. See the probe table.
12. **`GeoError::SubdivisionCountMismatch`** replaces `UnsupportedResolution` at
    the `verts.len() != D` guard (CodeRabbit and rust-dev, independently). `D`
    is already on the ladder when that fires, so the old variant printed "D must
    be 12, 42 or 162" when `D` *was* one of those — and it is the guard keeping
    `copy_from_slice` from panicking, so it is the one place the message is read.
13. **`vertex_count` and `level_for` collapsed onto one `LADDER`.** See "Open".
14. **`Vec3` made public.** A `pub fn apply_mat` signed with a private alias
    renders as a bare `[f64; 3]` beside a linkable `Mat3`.
15. **The φ/2 justification was over-claimed.** Max entrywise deviation is not a
    similarity invariant — conjugating the group by 0.3 rad gives 0.717, not
    0.809 — so "a constant of the icosahedral group" is true only in the
    `(0, ±1, ±φ)` frame. Replaced with the basis-free bound
    `2√2·sin36°/3 = 0.554`, which holds in *any* orientation.
16. **The portability inventory omitted `f64::midpoint`**, the one operation in
    the file IEEE-754 does not specify. Now named, with the cross-ISA
    measurement (identical bit dump on aarch64 and x86-64, including
    `-C target-cpu=x86-64-v3`; zero FMA-class instructions emitted).
17. **Two tolerance comments compared the wrong quantity** — a linear spacing
    against a squared tolerance, and "headroom over zero". Both now state the
    two measured squared distances that actually bracket the tolerance.
18. **"ulp at 1.0" was used in both directions** 300 lines apart (4.44e-16 as
    "2 ulp", 1.11e-16 as "one ulp"). Both correct, jointly implying two values
    for ulp(1.0). Now "above" and "below".
19. **Three unbacked or overstated claims of mine were withdrawn**: that Task 10
    needs `is_identity` (its plan section asks for neither it nor
    `rotation_matrices`); that a second copy is "the one thing that must never
    happen" (`borbax_universe::packing` holds a third, deliberately, as an
    independent oracle); and the `vertex_count` deferral, wrong on both the
    threshold and the cost.
20. **A coupling nobody had written down**: the `-0.0`/NaN test inspects `dirs`
    but licenses an exemption about the sort's *input*, which is sound only
    because `copy_from_slice` is the last step. Commented at the site.

#### The probes, and what each one discriminates

Run before the commit, per the standing rule that a guard nobody has watched
fail is not a guard. Each mutation was confirmed to have actually applied.

| Mutation | Tests that fail | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| `perms.rotate_left(1)` — reorder the table against the matrices | **1**: the geometry-tie test | Its doc claims to be the only check tying integers to rotations. Confirmed: every group-theoretic property survives a reordering, because the *set* is unchanged. |
| `R → -R` — build the 60 **improper** elements | **7**, incl. `minus_identity_is_not_in_the_group`, `identity_is_present`, `permutations_form_a_group` | The hazard CLAUDE.md names, caught at this task's level rather than Task 10's. Note the improper elements are a coset, not a group, so closure fails too. |
| plant `-0.0` in a direction | **1**: the new no-negative-zero test | The important result. `-0.0` sorts before `+0.0` under `total_cmp`, so this silently reorders the whole direction set — and **17 tests pass through it**, because every other property is order-invariant. Nothing else in the suite can see it. |
| `is_identity` TOL `1e-9 → 1e-17` | 1: the new is_identity test | Below the identity's own 4.44e-16 construction error → selects none. |
| `is_identity` TOL `1e-9 → 1.0` | 1: the new is_identity test | Above φ/2 = cos 36°, the nearest real rotation → selects more than one. Probed on both sides, per the recorded rule that the far miss is the one that lulls you. |
| one ulp on `dirs[0][0]` (~1.1e-16) | 1: the antipode involution | Proves item 10 bought something: this is three orders inside the `< 1e-12` the assertion used to carry, and under `build_anti`'s own `1e-24` on the squared distance, so construction still succeeds silently. |
| `normalise`: `scale(a, 1.0 / n)` → componentwise division | **1**: `the_canonical_direction_ordering_is_pinned` | Added after review. Mathematically identical, *better* rounded, unflagged by clippy, and how `borbax_universe::packing`'s copy already spells it — yet it moves six of 162 index positions at D=162. Before the golden, the whole 325-test workspace stayed green. |
| re-key the sort `(z,y,x)` → `(x,y,z)` | **1**: the same golden | Full relabelling of the direction set. Also 325/325 green beforehand. |

**The probe above the line was the weaker half of a pair, and review found the
stronger half.** The one-ulp probe perturbs `dirs[0][0]` *after* the sort, where
it breaks antipodal symmetry and is caught. A one-ulp change *upstream* of the
sort preserves antipodal symmetry — every operation involved is sign-symmetric —
and was caught by nothing, because 121 of the 161 adjacent pairs at D=162 share
a bit-identical `z` and the tie is broken only by rounding. Two review lanes
found this independently, from different instruments.

#### Routed forward, written into the task that executes it

- **Task 8 or 9 — §22.8 has no test anywhere in V0.** The spec justifies
  searching 60 rotations rather than 120 on the grounds that a molecule and its
  mirror image are genuinely different species. Task 7 discharged the
  precondition (`minus_identity_is_not_in_the_group` proves `anti ∉ perms`), but
  every `canonicalise` test in Task 9 asserts rotation-*invariance* and none
  asserts that a chiral molecule and its enantiomer canonicalise *differently*.
  If they ever collided the two would intern as one species and homochirality
  would be impossible whatever the kernel does. Grepped: no such test at any
  task. Needs a fixture asserted chiral — not `chain(6)`, which may not be.

- **Task 10 — the required probe, and why the obvious fixture is worse than
  useless.** See CLAUDE.md's ANTI bullet for the measurement: at D=42 a
  same-index complement scores −4.602147 against the correct kernel and
  **0.000000** against the broken one, so it rewards the defect. Task 10 must
  carry: delete `anti[...]` from `affinity`'s index expression and **both**
  `a_physical_complement_scores_zero` and `the_mirror_complement_does_not_fit`
  must fail. If only one does, the pair is not discriminating. Also assert
  `real_signature`'s fixture is chiral before using it to test handedness.

- **Task 20 — `borbax_universe::packing`'s independent oracle disagrees with the
  shipped table** on six of 162 index positions at D=162, because its
  `normalise` divides componentwise where the molecule copy multiplies by the
  reciprocal. Harmless in itself — it is `#[cfg(test)]` and deliberately
  independent — but the open question is whether `FRONTIER_COEFF`'s admitted
  band was located against a latitude ordering the shipped geodesic does not
  reproduce. Discriminator: re-run the packing probe with the
  reciprocal-multiply form and compare the band. Written into `packing.rs`'s own
  doc comment as well.

#### Open, accepted

- **§22.2 sweeps and locks `D`.** All group properties, the antipode table and
  the geometry tie are now checked at 12, 42 and 162, so the lock can land on
  any of the three without a level-dependent surprise. Nothing else here
  assumes 42.
- **`vertex_count` overflowed, and the deferral written here was wrong twice.**
  It claimed `level ≥ 32` (the first overflowing level is **31**) and justified
  leaving it by the cost to "every call site" — of which, measured, there were
  **zero** outside its own test. At `level = 32` release wrapping returned
  **`2`**: a plausible wrong answer from a `pub` function. Fixed rather than
  deferred, by collapsing `vertex_count` and `level_for` onto one `LADDER`
  table so they cannot disagree and a lookup cannot overflow.

---

### Task 8: 3D embedding by stress majorization

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/borbax-molecule/src/layout.rs`
- Test: same file

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Mol12`, `borbax_universe::Universe`
- Produces: `Embedding` (positions + count), `embed(&Mol12, &Universe) -> Embedding`

**ROUTED FROM TASK 6's REVIEW (geometry lane), then substantially corrected by
that same lane on cross-check. Read the corrections — the first version's
mechanism and its discriminator were both wrong, and a wrong discriminator in a
plan is the green-test-over-a-dead-mechanism shape this project keeps recording.**

**What survives, and it is the whole finding: Task 6 hands Task 8 no canonical
vertex order.** `canonicalise` returns `(CanonForm, SearchStats)` and discards
the ordering that made the form canonical. There is no `CanonForm -> Mol12`, and
`pair_bit` — the only thing that can decode `CanonForm::planes` back to atom
pairs — is private to `canonical.rs`. So `embed(&Mol12, ...)` receives the
*caller's* labelling with no way to reach the species' own.

**Withdrawn on measurement, so nobody re-derives them:**

- *"SMACOF's initialisation is per-vertex-index."* False about the code this task
  lifts from: `experiments/src/embed.rs` already keys every pivot tie-break and
  the jitter seed off `mol.canonical_order()`.
- *"One species id and two different 3D shapes."* Measured false **for trees**.
  Over 300 relabellings of random trees (n = 4..12) the *shape* — the sorted
  multiset of pairwise distances, invariant to labelling and rigid motion —
  agreed to 3.3e-13, and stress to 8.3e-13. Per-atom coordinates differ by up to
  4.0 on 298 of 300, but that is an automorphism relabelling of the *same* rigid
  configuration, not a second shape.

**And the caveat that makes the withdrawal honest: the corpus was trees only, and
not incidentally.** `experiments`' `canonical_form` is the AHU tree encoding — it
recurses filtering only the parent, so it does not terminate correctly on a
cycle, and every generator in that crate is a path, a star or a random tree.
`Mol12` admits rings. The twelve-cycle and the Petersen fixture in Task 6 are
exactly the large-automorphism graphs where a labelling-dependent layout would
bite. **So the reassuring measurement covers the graphs that do not matter and
says nothing about the ones that do.**

*Discriminator — state the indexing convention first, because it decides which
property is even correct:*

- If `Embedding` is indexed by **canonical position**, `embed` must be
  **bit-identical** across 20 random relabellings.
- If it is indexed by the **caller's atom index**, the property is
  **equivariance** — `embed(m.relabelled(p))[p[i]] == embed(m)[i]`, bitwise —
  and bit-identity is the *wrong* assertion, which a correct implementation
  would fail.
- Either way add the check neither convention can dodge: **the sorted multiset of
  pairwise distances must agree bitwise.** On the `experiments` reference only
  that third check currently passes.

*Property a fix must have:* the embedding is a function of the species, not of
the caller. Either Task 6's `canonicalise` grows a way to hand back the ordering
(or a total `CanonForm -> Mol12` inverse, with
`canonicalise(mol_of(canonicalise(m).0)).0 == canonicalise(m).0` as its test), or
`embed` canonicalises internally before laying anything out. Do **not** re-derive
`pair_bit`'s inverse in `layout.rs` — a second copy of that formula is a silent
mismatch waiting to happen, and its being private is the statement that it should
have exactly one home.

*Residual for `determinism-auditor` at this task, sized by nobody yet:* even from
a bitwise-equal start, `smacof` diverges under relabelling on 239 of 241 cases,
worst 1.9e-12, because `guttman_step`'s inner accumulation and `centre`'s sum are
both in **caller index order**. `embed.rs` already names this hazard but scopes
its threat model to threads; relabelling reorders that sum just as surely.
Whether 1e-12 can flip an affinity comparison depends on Task 9's quantisation
and Task 10's tie-breaks, neither of which exists yet.

**From the Task 2 review — a live hazard in the code this task lifts from.**
`experiments/src/embed.rs` picks SMACOF's landmark pivots with `max_by` over
`graph_distances`, which returns `f64::INFINITY` for an unreachable atom. It is
safe in `experiments` only because the test molecules are random trees. On a
graph with a disconnected component the pivots become infinite, stress hits
`inf - inf`, the whole embedding goes NaN, and NaN then wins every downstream
selection — a species with a garbage signature and a garbage affinity, with
nothing failing anywhere. Guard it here: either `graph_distances` returns a
`Result` on a disconnected input, or `embed` asserts finiteness before the
pivot selection. The distinguishing test is a two-component graph asserting the
result is an error or entirely finite; add a two-atom molecule and a path graph
while you are there, since those are the other degenerate SMACOF inputs.

**And decide the `Span` question below before writing `Signature`, because
retrofitting it afterwards is a much larger diff.**

Every length in the geometric core is currently planned as a bare `f64` —
`Signature.r`, `BindConsts::ideal_gap`, the cavity `SHELL`, `LATTICE_SPAN` —
while `Element::radius` is already a `Span` that gets `.get()`-ed at the first
arithmetic site. So the one dimension where a unit mix-up has already happened
*twice in this project* is the one passing through untyped:

- **G1**, still open: `ideal_gap` is on the wrong length scale, so binding is
  ~93% size and ~7% shape — complementarity never selects anything.
- The chemistry plan's own cavity comment describes the same bug in the past
  tense: adding raw grid integers to atom radii and comparing against
  `ideal_gap`, "two incommensurable numbers".

Both would have been compile errors with `Span`. `a` (surface character) stays
`f64` — it is genuinely dimensionless. Note `ds * ds` will not type-check once
`ds` is a `Span`, which is correct: a squared length is a different quantity,
and `w_shape` carries the reciprocal. Whether that wants a typed weight or a
`.get()` at the accumulator is the design call to make here.

The distinguishing test is a `compile_fail` doctest of the shape "a lattice
cell offset added to an atom radius does not compile", written against the
actual `LATTICE_SPAN`/`radius` spellings — typing `Signature.r` alone would
pass a test written only about `Signature`.

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use borbax_rng::{Domain, Stream};
    use borbax_universe::{ElementId, Universe};

    fn chain(n: u8) -> Mol12 {
        let mut m = Mol12::new();
        for i in 0..n {
            m.add_atom(ElementId(i % 3));
        }
        for i in 0..n - 1 {
            m.add_bond(i, i + 1, 1);
        }
        m
    }

    #[test]
    fn embedding_is_deterministic() {
        let u = Universe::generate(4);
        assert_eq!(embed(&chain(7), &u).pos, embed(&chain(7), &u).pos);
    }

    #[test]
    fn positions_are_finite_and_centred() {
        let u = Universe::generate(4);
        let e = embed(&chain(9), &u);
        assert!(e.pos[..e.n].iter().all(|p| p.iter().all(|c| c.is_finite())));
        let mut sum = [0.0; 3];
        for p in &e.pos[..e.n] {
            for k in 0..3 {
                sum[k] += p[k];
            }
        }
        assert!(sum.iter().all(|c| c.abs() < 1e-9), "not centred: {sum:?}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn bonded_atoms_end_up_closer_than_distant_ones() {
        let u = Universe::generate(4);
        let e = embed(&chain(8), &u);
        let d = |a: usize, b: usize| {
            let (p, q) = (e.pos[a], e.pos[b]);
            ((p[0] - q[0]).powi(2) + (p[1] - q[1]).powi(2) + (p[2] - q[2]).powi(2)).sqrt()
        };
        assert!(d(0, 1) < d(0, 4), "graph distance not reflected in geometry");
        assert!(d(0, 4) < d(0, 7));
    }

    /// **The load-bearing property of the whole design.** A small change to
    /// the graph must produce a small change to the geometry, or mutation
    /// becomes a random walk in shape space and nothing can accumulate.
    /// Spec §8.2 calls this out as what makes the chemistry evolvable.
    #[test]
    fn small_graph_changes_give_small_geometry_changes() {
        let u = Universe::generate(4);
        let mut rng = Stream::new(31, Domain::Molecule, 0);

        // Over random *trees*, not one straight chain. A chain is the easiest
        // possible case and passed the earlier version of this test at 0.45
        // while 135 of 200 random trees exceeded the same bound — the frame
        // was reorienting and a chain does not reveal it.
        let (mut worst_unaligned, mut worst_aligned) = (0.0f64, 0.0f64);
        for _ in 0..200 {
            let m = random_tree(&mut rng, 8);
            let base = embed(&m, &u);

            let mut grown = m;
            let Some(new_atom) = grown.add_atom(ElementId(1)) else { continue };
            grown.add_bond(rng.next_range(8) as u8, new_atom, 1);
            let after = embed(&grown, &u);

            let extent = base.radius_of_gyration().max(1e-9);
            for i in 0..8 {
                let (p, q) = (base.pos[i], after.pos[i]);
                let d = ((p[0] - q[0]).powi(2) + (p[1] - q[1]).powi(2) + (p[2] - q[2]).powi(2)).sqrt();
                worst_unaligned = worst_unaligned.max(d / extent);
            }
            worst_aligned = worst_aligned.max(kabsch_rmsd(&base, &after, 8) / extent);
        }

        // Both bounds, deliberately. `aligned` alone tests that SMACOF finds a
        // stable *shape*; `unaligned` tests that it lands in a stable *frame*,
        // which is the property signature canonicalisation cannot recover
        // (§8.2). Passing the first while failing the second is exactly the
        // bug this replaced.
        assert!(worst_aligned < 0.6, "shape unstable: {worst_aligned}");
        assert!(worst_unaligned < 0.6, "frame unstable: {worst_unaligned}");
    }

    /// Relabelling must not change the embedding up to atom correspondence.
    /// Nothing tested this, and `embed` reads `m.elem` positionally — so if it
    /// is ever called on a non-canonical `Mol12` the result depends on input
    /// labelling, and species identity goes with it.
    #[test]
    fn embedding_is_invariant_under_relabelling() {
        let u = Universe::generate(4);
        let mut rng = Stream::new(41, Domain::Molecule, 0);
        for _ in 0..200 {
            let m = random_tree(&mut rng, 7);
            let mut p: Vec<u8> = (0..m.n).collect();
            for i in (1..p.len()).rev() {
                p.swap(i, rng.next_range((i + 1) as u64) as usize);
            }
            let a = embed(&m, &u);
            let b = embed(&permute(&m, &p), &u);
            // Same multiset of pairwise distances, in the permuted order.
            for i in 0..m.n as usize {
                for j in 0..m.n as usize {
                    let da = dist(a.pos[p[i] as usize], a.pos[p[j] as usize]);
                    let db = dist(b.pos[i], b.pos[j]);
                    assert!((da - db).abs() < 1e-9, "relabelling changed the geometry");
                }
            }
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn handles_degenerate_inputs() {
        let u = Universe::generate(4);
        let mut single = Mol12::new();
        single.add_atom(ElementId(0));
        assert_eq!(embed(&single, &u).n, 1);
        assert_eq!(embed(&Mol12::new(), &u).n, 0);
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the embedding**

```rust
//! 3D embedding by stress majorization (spec §8.2).
//!
//! Laplacian eigenvectors were the obvious choice and are wrong: eigenvectors
//! have arbitrary sign, and degenerate eigenvalues are common in the small
//! symmetric graphs molecules actually are, leaving the basis arbitrary. Since
//! the signature keys species identity and the fold cache, that arbitrariness
//! would land exactly where it does most damage. Stress majorization from a
//! deterministic start sidesteps the problem rather than managing it.
//!
//! No randomness is used at all — the initial placement is a function of the
//! atom index, so the result depends only on the molecule.

use crate::graph::{Mol12, MAX_ATOMS};
use borbax_universe::{ElementId, Universe};

/// Fixed iteration count. Not a convergence test: a tolerance-based stop
/// would make the result depend on floating-point details that vary in the
/// last bits, and §13.4 requires the same answer on every platform.
const ITERATIONS: usize = 240;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Embedding {
    pub n: usize,
    pub pos: [[f64; 3]; MAX_ATOMS],
}

impl Embedding {
    /// RMS distance from the centroid — the molecule's own length scale,
    /// used to express displacements as fractions rather than absolutes.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn radius_of_gyration(&self) -> f64 {
        if self.n == 0 {
            return 0.0;
        }
        let s: f64 = self.pos[..self.n].iter().map(|p| p[0] * p[0] + p[1] * p[1] + p[2] * p[2]).sum();
        (s / self.n as f64).sqrt()
    }
}

/// All-pairs shortest path by BFS. n <= 12, so this is trivially cheap and
/// there is no reason to reach for anything cleverer.
fn hop_distances(m: &Mol12) -> [[u8; MAX_ATOMS]; MAX_ATOMS] {
    let n = m.n as usize;
    let mut d = [[u8::MAX; MAX_ATOMS]; MAX_ATOMS];
    for s in 0..n {
        d[s][s] = 0;
        let mut frontier: u16 = 1 << s;
        let mut seen: u16 = frontier;
        let mut depth = 0u8;
        while frontier != 0 {
            depth += 1;
            let mut next: u16 = 0;
            for v in 0..n {
                if frontier & (1 << v) != 0 {
                    next |= m.neighbours(v as u8);
                }
            }
            next &= !seen;
            seen |= next;
            for v in 0..n {
                if next & (1 << v) != 0 {
                    d[s][v] = depth;
                }
            }
            frontier = next;
        }
    }
    // Disconnected pairs: treat as one hop beyond the graph's diameter so
    // fragments are pushed apart without the solver seeing an infinity.
    let diameter = d.iter().flatten().filter(|x| **x != u8::MAX).copied().max().unwrap_or(1);
    for row in d.iter_mut().take(n) {
        for cell in row.iter_mut().take(n) {
            if *cell == u8::MAX {
                *cell = diameter.saturating_add(1);
            }
        }
    }
    d
}

#[must_use]
pub fn embed(m: &Mol12, u: &Universe) -> Embedding {
    let n = m.n as usize;
    let mut pos = [[0.0f64; 3]; MAX_ATOMS];
    if n == 0 {
        return Embedding { n: 0, pos };
    }
    if n == 1 {
        return Embedding { n: 1, pos };
    }

    // Target distance for one bond: the sum of the two atoms' radii, so
    // bigger atoms genuinely take up more room.
    let radius = |i: usize| u.element(ElementId(m.elem[i])).radius.get();
    let hops = hop_distances(m);
    let mut target = [[0.0f64; MAX_ATOMS]; MAX_ATOMS];
    for i in 0..n {
        for j in 0..n {
            if i != j {
                target[i][j] = f64::from(hops[i][j]) * (radius(i) + radius(j));
            }
        }
    }

    // Deterministic start. Spreads atoms out so the solver does not begin in
    // a degenerate configuration; any fixed start works.
    //
    // No trigonometry: a spiral needs `cos`/`sin`, which are platform-
    // dependent (§13.1), and the start does not need to be a good sphere —
    // only spread out and reproducible. These rational offsets are neither
    // collinear nor coplanar, which is all the solver requires.
    const START: [[f64; 3]; MAX_ATOMS] = [
        [1.0, 0.0, 0.0], [-1.0, 0.25, 0.5], [0.25, 1.0, -0.5], [-0.5, -1.0, 0.25],
        [0.5, 0.25, 1.0], [-0.25, 0.5, -1.0], [1.0, -0.75, -0.25], [-1.0, -0.5, 0.75],
        [0.75, 1.0, 0.5], [-0.75, 0.5, -1.0], [0.5, -1.0, 0.75], [-0.5, 0.75, 1.0],
    ];
    // Mean over *all* pairs, not row 0 — one atom's row is not the molecule's
    // length scale, and using it makes the start depend on which atom is first.
    let mut sum = 0.0;
    let mut cnt = 0.0;
    for i in 0..n {
        for j in 0..n {
            if i != j {
                sum += target[i][j];
                cnt += 1.0;
            }
        }
    }
    let mean_target = if cnt > 0.0 { sum / cnt } else { 1.0 };
    for i in 0..n {
        for k in 0..3 {
            pos[i][k] = START[i][k] * mean_target;
        }
    }

    // SMACOF. Weight 1/d^2 makes short (bonded) distances dominate, which is
    // what keeps local structure faithful while letting the global shape relax.
    let mut next = pos;
    for _ in 0..ITERATIONS {
        for i in 0..n {
            let mut num = [0.0f64; 3];
            let mut den = 0.0f64;
            for j in 0..n {
                if i == j {
                    continue;
                }
                let dij = target[i][j];
                if dij <= 0.0 {
                    continue;
                }
                let diff = [pos[i][0] - pos[j][0], pos[i][1] - pos[j][1], pos[i][2] - pos[j][2]];
                let dist = (diff[0] * diff[0] + diff[1] * diff[1] + diff[2] * diff[2]).sqrt().max(1e-9);
                let w = 1.0 / (dij * dij);
                for k in 0..3 {
                    num[k] += w * (pos[j][k] + dij * diff[k] / dist);
                }
                den += w;
            }
            if den > 0.0 {
                for k in 0..3 {
                    next[i][k] = num[k] / den;
                }
            }
        }
        pos = next;
    }

    // Centre on the centroid. Fixed summation order (spec §13.1).
    let mut c = [0.0f64; 3];
    for p in pos.iter().take(n) {
        for k in 0..3 {
            c[k] += p[k];
        }
    }
    for k in 0..3 {
        c[k] /= n as f64;
    }
    for p in pos.iter_mut().take(n) {
        for k in 0..3 {
            p[k] -= c[k];
        }
    }

    canonicalise_frame(&mut pos, n);
    Embedding { n, pos }
}

/// Rotate the embedding into a frame derived from the canonical atom order.
///
/// **Without this the shape is stable but the orientation is not**, and the
/// difference matters more than it sounds. Measured over 200 random tree
/// edits: aligned RMSD/R_g has median 0.13 — SMACOF does its job — but
/// *unaligned* displacement has median 0.74 and p90 1.34. Adding one atom
/// reorients the whole configuration by a generic rotation.
///
/// Canonicalising the signature over 60 rotations (§8.2) cannot absorb that:
/// it removes rotations *in the icosahedral group*, and a generic SO(3)
/// rotation sits up to ~44° from the nearest group element. So a one-atom
/// mutation would move the canonical signature by an amount dominated by an
/// arbitrary reorientation rather than by the shape change — mutation becomes
/// a random walk in signature space, neutral networks never form, and
/// `affinity` acquires a large pseudo-random component from two arbitrary
/// frames.
///
/// A straight chain does not show this, which is why the original
/// `chain(8) -> chain(9)` test passed at 0.45 while 135 of 200 random trees
/// exceeded the same threshold.
///
/// The frame is built from the lowest-indexed atoms that are non-degenerate,
/// and *which* atoms those are is part of the physics: the input must already
/// be in canonical order.
fn canonicalise_frame(pos: &mut [[f64; 3]; MAX_ATOMS], n: usize) {
    if n < 2 {
        return;
    }
    let norm = |v: [f64; 3]| (v[0] * v[0] + v[1] * v[1] + v[2] * v[2]).sqrt();
    let scale_by = 1.0f64.max(norm(pos[0]));

    // e0: first atom far enough from the centroid to define a direction.
    let Some(a0) = (0..n).find(|&i| norm(pos[i]) > 1e-9 * scale_by) else {
        return; // everything coincident; nothing to orient
    };
    let d0 = norm(pos[a0]);
    let e0 = [pos[a0][0] / d0, pos[a0][1] / d0, pos[a0][2] / d0];

    // e1: first atom not near-collinear with e0, orthogonalised against it.
    let mut e1 = [0.0f64; 3];
    let mut found = false;
    for i in 0..n {
        let p = pos[i];
        let dot = p[0] * e0[0] + p[1] * e0[1] + p[2] * e0[2];
        let perp = [p[0] - dot * e0[0], p[1] - dot * e0[1], p[2] - dot * e0[2]];
        let m = norm(perp);
        if m > 1e-9 * scale_by {
            e1 = [perp[0] / m, perp[1] / m, perp[2] / m];
            found = true;
            break;
        }
    }
    if !found {
        return; // collinear molecule; the axis alone is already canonical
    }
    let e2 = [
        e0[1] * e1[2] - e0[2] * e1[1],
        e0[2] * e1[0] - e0[0] * e1[2],
        e0[0] * e1[1] - e0[1] * e1[0],
    ];

    for p in pos.iter_mut().take(n) {
        let v = *p;
        *p = [
            v[0] * e0[0] + v[1] * e0[1] + v[2] * e0[2],
            v[0] * e1[0] + v[1] * e1[1] + v[2] * e1[2],
            v[0] * e2[0] + v[1] * e2[1] + v[2] * e2[2],
        ];
    }
}
```

not accumulate anything. Raise `ITERATIONS` or revisit the weighting first.

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::{graph::Mol12, layout::embed};
    use borbax_universe::{ElementId, Universe};

    fn sig_of(m: &Mol12, u: &Universe, g: &Geodesic<42>) -> Signature<42> {
        signature(m, &embed(m, u), g, u)
    }

    fn chain(n: u8) -> Mol12 {
        let mut m = Mol12::new();
        for i in 0..n {
            m.add_atom(ElementId(i % 3));
        }
        for i in 0..n - 1 {
            m.add_bond(i, i + 1, 1);
        }
        m
    }

    #[test]
    fn is_deterministic() {
        let (u, g) = (Universe::generate(6), Geodesic::<42>::build().unwrap());
        assert_eq!(sig_of(&chain(6), &u, &g), sig_of(&chain(6), &u, &g));
    }

    #[test]
    fn extents_are_positive_and_finite() {
        let (u, g) = (Universe::generate(6), Geodesic::<42>::build().unwrap());
        let s = sig_of(&chain(7), &u, &g);
        assert!(s.r.iter().all(|x| x.is_finite() && *x > 0.0));
        assert!(s.a.iter().all(|x| x.is_finite() && (-1.0..=1.0).contains(x)));
    }

    /// The canonical form must be invariant under the rotation group. If it
    /// is not, the same molecule can intern as two species depending on how
    /// its embedding happened to land (spec §8.2).
    #[test]
    fn canonical_form_is_rotation_invariant() {
        let (u, g) = (Universe::generate(6), Geodesic::<42>::build().unwrap());
        let s = sig_of(&chain(6), &u, &g).canonicalise(&g);
        for r in 0..N_ROTATIONS {
            assert_eq!(s.permuted(&g.perms[r]).canonicalise(&g), s, "rotation {r} broke invariance");
        }
    }

    /// The evolvability property again, now at the signature level. A one-atom
    /// change must move the signature far less than an unrelated molecule does.
    #[test]
    fn small_changes_move_the_signature_less_than_unrelated_molecules() {
        let (u, g) = (Universe::generate(6), Geodesic::<42>::build().unwrap());
        let base = sig_of(&chain(8), &u, &g);

        let mut grown = chain(8);
        grown.add_atom(ElementId(1));
        grown.add_bond(7, 8, 1);
        let near = base.distance(&sig_of(&grown, &u, &g));

        let mut other = Mol12::new();
        for i in 0..8 {
            other.add_atom(ElementId(i % 3));
        }
        for i in 0..7 {
            other.add_bond(0, i + 1, 1); // a star, not a chain
        }
        let far = base.distance(&sig_of(&other, &u, &g));

        assert!(near < far, "one-atom change ({near}) not closer than a different shape ({far})");
    }

    #[test]
    fn sorted_extents_are_rotation_invariant() {
        let (u, g) = (Universe::generate(6), Geodesic::<42>::build().unwrap());
        let s = sig_of(&chain(6), &u, &g);
        for r in 0..N_ROTATIONS {
            assert_eq!(s.permuted(&g.perms[r]).sorted_extents(), s.sorted_extents());
        }
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the signature**

```rust
//! The shape signature (spec §8.2) — the heart of the engine.
//!
//! A molecule reduces to two numbers per sample direction: how far its
//! surface extends that way, and what surface character faces that way.
//! Everything downstream — binding, catalysis, membranes, permeability — is
//! a comparison between two of these.

use crate::geodesic::{Geodesic, N_ROTATIONS};
use crate::graph::Mol12;
use crate::layout::Embedding;
use borbax_universe::{ElementId, Universe};

/// Width of the surface shell that contributes to surface character.
/// Atoms within this of the supporting plane are "facing" the direction.
const SHELL: f64 = 0.75;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Signature<const D: usize> {
    /// Surface extent per direction — the support function of the atom spheres.
    pub r: [f64; D],
    /// Summed surface character per direction, in [-1, 1].
    pub a: [f64; D],
}

impl<const D: usize> Signature<D> {
    #[must_use]
    pub fn zeroed() -> Self {
        Self { r: [0.0; D], a: [0.0; D] }
    }

    /// Apply a rotation, given as a permutation of directions.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn permuted(&self, perm: &[u8; D]) -> Self {
        let mut out = Self::zeroed();
        for i in 0..D {
            out.r[perm[i] as usize] = self.r[i];
            out.a[perm[i] as usize] = self.a[i];
        }
        out
    }

    /// The lexicographically smallest of this signature's 60 rotations.
    ///
    /// Without this the same molecule could produce different signatures
    /// depending on how its embedding happened to land, and species identity
    /// would stop being well-defined. `total_cmp` gives a total order on f64
    /// with no tolerance and no platform variation.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn canonicalise(&self, g: &Geodesic<D>) -> Self {
        let mut best = self.permuted(&g.perms[0]);
        for r in 1..N_ROTATIONS {
            let cand = self.permuted(&g.perms[r]);
            if cand.lex_cmp(&best) == std::cmp::Ordering::Less {
                best = cand;
            }
        }
        best
    }

    fn lex_cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> std::cmp::Ordering {
        for i in 0..D {
            match self.r[i].total_cmp(&other.r[i]) {
                std::cmp::Ordering::Equal => {}
                ord => return ord,
            }
        }
        for i in 0..D {
            match self.a[i].total_cmp(&other.a[i]) {
                std::cmp::Ordering::Equal => {}
                ord => return ord,
            }
        }
        std::cmp::Ordering::Equal
    }

    /// Rotation-invariant summary, used as a cheap pre-filter before the full
    /// binding search (spec §8.3). Any rotation permutes the extents, so the
    /// sorted multiset is unchanged.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn sorted_extents(&self) -> Vec<f64> {
        let mut v = self.r.to_vec();
        v.sort_by(f64::total_cmp);
        v
    }

    /// Euclidean distance in signature space. Used by the novelty metric and
    /// by the stability tests; not on any hot path.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn distance(&self, other: &Self) -> f64 {
        let mut acc = 0.0;
        for i in 0..D {
            acc += (self.r[i] - other.r[i]).powi(2) + (self.a[i] - other.a[i]).powi(2);
        }
        acc.sqrt()
    }
}

/// Build a signature from an embedded molecule.
///
/// `r` is the support function of the union of atom spheres: the furthest the
/// surface reaches in each direction. `a` is the surface character averaged
/// over the atoms that actually face that way, weighted linearly by how close
/// they sit to the supporting plane.
///
/// The weighting is deliberately polynomial rather than an exponential
/// falloff: `exp` is not portable between platform libm implementations
/// (spec §13.1), and a linear shell gives the same qualitative behaviour
/// with no reproducibility risk.
#[must_use]
pub fn signature<const D: usize>(
    m: &Mol12,
    e: &Embedding,
    g: &Geodesic<D>,
    u: &Universe,
) -> Signature<D> {
    let mut out = Signature::<D>::zeroed();
    if e.n == 0 {
        return out;
    }

    for d in 0..D {
        let dir = g.dirs[d];

        // Support function: the furthest any atom's surface reaches this way.
        let mut extent = f64::NEG_INFINITY;
        for i in 0..e.n {
            let el = u.element(ElementId(m.elem[i]));
            let reach = e.pos[i][0] * dir[0] + e.pos[i][1] * dir[1] + e.pos[i][2] * dir[2]
                + el.radius.get();
            if reach > extent {
                extent = reach;
            }
        }

        // Surface character: atoms within SHELL of the supporting plane,
        // weighted by how close they are to it. Fixed iteration order, so the
        // summation order is fixed (spec §13.1).
        let mut num = 0.0;
        let mut den = 0.0;
        for i in 0..e.n {
            let el = u.element(ElementId(m.elem[i]));
            let reach = e.pos[i][0] * dir[0] + e.pos[i][1] * dir[1] + e.pos[i][2] * dir[2]
                + el.radius.get();
            let w = (reach - (extent - SHELL)).max(0.0);
            num += w * el.affinity;
            den += w;
        }

        out.r[d] = extent;
        out.a[d] = if den > 0.0 { (num / den).clamp(-1.0, 1.0) } else { 0.0 };
    }
    out
}
```

```bash
git add crates/borbax-molecule/src/signature.rs
git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'
feat(molecule): directional shape signature

Extent is the support function of the atom spheres; surface character is
the affinity of atoms facing that way, weighted linearly by proximity to
the supporting plane. The weighting is polynomial rather than an
exponential falloff on purpose — exp is not portable between platform libm
implementations (§13.1), and a linear shell behaves the same qualitatively
with no reproducibility risk.

Signatures are rotation-canonicalised to the lexicographically smallest of
their 60 rotations, using total_cmp so the ordering is total and
platform-independent. Without this the same molecule could intern as two
species depending on how its embedding landed.

Rotation invariance is tested against all 60 rotations rather than spot
checked, and the evolvability property is asserted at signature level: a
one-atom change must move the signature less than an unrelated shape does.

MrReasonable <4990954+MrReasonable@users.noreply.github.com>
EOF
)"
```

### Task 10: The binding kernel

**Carried in from Task 4's review — `affinity` carries no drawn information and
its trend direction is fixed.** §7.1 line 288 says "the sign of each trend is
drawn from the seed, so one universe has affinity rising across a period and
another has it falling. This costs nothing and is the difference between
'invented' and 'renamed'." Measured over 500 universes: affinity falls across a
period in **1020 of 1020** cases, and `(k, units) -> affinity` has **0 bit
mismatches**, so no drawn constant enters the formula at all.

Separately, the attained range is **[0.140476, 1.000000]** — strictly positive,
exhaustively over the `(k, units)` grid. §8.3's charge term `-(a_A + a_B)^2` is
maximised at zero when the two affinities are *opposite*, so an all-positive
range degenerates it from a complementarity test into a monotone penalty on
total surface affinity, and §5's membrane mechanism — one flank positive, the
opposite negative — is unreachable.

How `a` is summed from element affinities is **this task's** code, so the remedy
may belong here rather than in Task 4: if the sum is centred or mean-subtracted,
the problem dissolves. What must not happen is the range being inherited by
default. `affinity_is_in_range_and_continuous` asserts the attained bounds on
both sides, so it fails loudly the moment either moves.

*Discriminator:* the attained affinity set reaching the binding kernel must
straddle zero over the enumerable `(k, units)` grid, and the test must assert the
attained range rather than containment in `[-1, 1]` — a containment assertion
passes for a collapse as well as for correctness, which is how the old
`> -1.0` bound sat with 1.14 of slack and could not fire.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/borbax-molecule/src/binding.rs`
- Modify: `crates/borbax-molecule/src/lib.rs` (re-exports)
- Test: same file

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Signature<D>`, `Geodesic<D>`, `borbax_universe::UniverseConsts`
- Produces: `BindConsts`, `affinity<D>(&Signature<D>, &Signature<D>, &Geodesic<D>, &BindConsts) -> f64`, `may_bind<D>(...) -> bool`, `bind_probability(f64, Thermal) -> f64`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::geodesic::Geodesic;

    fn consts() -> BindConsts {
        BindConsts { ideal_gap: 2.0, w_shape: 1.0, w_charge: 1.0 }
    }

    /// A signature and its exact complement: extents summing to the ideal gap,
    /// characters summing to zero. This is the definition of a perfect fit, so
    /// it must score above anything else we can construct.
    /// Build the **physical** complement of `a`: the shape that would actually
    /// fit against it.
    ///
    /// The complement is constructed through `anti`, because surfaces meet
    /// along opposite directions. An earlier version of this helper built it
    /// at the *same* index — which made it a restatement of the implementation
    /// rather than a test of it, and it passed happily while the kernel was
    /// searching reflections instead of rotations.
    ///
    /// A test whose expected value is derived the same way as the code cannot
    /// detect that they are both wrong.
    fn complement_of<const D: usize>(
        a: &Signature<D>,
        g: &Geodesic<D>,
        k: &BindConsts,
    ) -> Signature<D> {
        let mut b = Signature::<D>::zeroed();
        for i in 0..D {
            let j = g.anti[i] as usize;
            b.r[i] = k.ideal_gap - a.r[j];
            b.a[i] = -a.a[j];
        }
        b
    }

    /// Seed from a real embedded molecule, not a synthetic pattern — a
    /// hand-made signature can satisfy an identity the geometry never
    /// produces.
    fn real_signature<const D: usize>(g: &Geodesic<D>, u: &Universe) -> Signature<D> {
        let mut m = Mol12::new();
        for i in 0..6 {
            m.add_atom(ElementId(i % 4));
        }
        for i in 0..5 {
            m.add_bond(i, i + 1, 1);
        }
        signature(&m, &embed(&m, u), g, u).canonicalise(g)
    }

    /// **The test that catches a missing `anti`.** A physical complement must
    /// score zero, which is the maximum since the score is a negated sum of
    /// squares. With `anti` omitted the kernel compares against the point
    /// inversion and this fails immediately.
    #[test]
    fn a_physical_complement_scores_zero() {
        let (u, g, k) = (Universe::generate(6), Geodesic::<42>::build().unwrap(), consts());
        let a = real_signature::<42>(&g, &u);
        let b = complement_of(&a, &g, &k);
        assert!(affinity(&a, &b, &g, &k).abs() < 1e-9, "the physical complement did not fit");
    }

    /// And the converse, which is what makes the first test meaningful: the
    /// complement built at the *same* index — the shape that fits the
    /// reflection — must score badly. If both pass, the kernel is not
    /// distinguishing a molecule from its mirror image at all.
    #[test]
    fn the_mirror_complement_does_not_fit() {
        let (u, g, k) = (Universe::generate(6), Geodesic::<42>::build().unwrap(), consts());
        let a = real_signature::<42>(&g, &u);
        let mut mirror = Signature::<42>::zeroed();
        for i in 0..42 {
            mirror.r[i] = k.ideal_gap - a.r[i];
            mirror.a[i] = -a.a[i];
        }
        assert!(
            affinity(&a, &mirror, &g, &k) < -1e-6,
            "the mirror complement fitted — the kernel is searching reflections"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn identical_bumpy_shapes_bind_worse_than_complements() {
        let (u, g, k) = (Universe::generate(6), Geodesic::<42>::build().unwrap(), consts());
        let a = real_signature::<42>(&g, &u);
        let b = complement_of(&a, &g, &k);
        assert!(affinity(&a, &b, &g, &k) > affinity(&a, &a, &g, &k), "bump met bump and won");
    }

    /// Symmetry holds mathematically because the permutation set is a group
    /// (closed under inverse) and the per-direction terms are symmetric. It
    /// holds only to within summation order in floating point, which is
    /// exactly why callers must order the pair — see `affinity_ordered`.
    #[test]
    fn affinity_is_symmetric_to_within_summation_order() {
        let (u, g, k) = (Universe::generate(6), Geodesic::<42>::build().unwrap(), consts());
        let a = real_signature::<42>(&g, &u);
        let b = complement_of(&a, &g, &k);
        let (x, y) = (affinity(&a, &b, &g, &k), affinity(&b, &a, &g, &k));
        assert!((x - y).abs() < 1e-9, "{x} vs {y}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn ordered_affinity_is_exactly_symmetric() {
        let (u, g, k) = (Universe::generate(6), Geodesic::<42>::build().unwrap(), consts());
        let a = real_signature::<42>(&g, &u);
        let b = complement_of(&a, &g, &k);
        // Bit-for-bit, not within a tolerance. This is the function the memo
        // and every caller must use.
        assert_eq!(affinity_ordered(&a, &b, &g, &k), affinity_ordered(&b, &a, &g, &k));
    }

    /// The pre-filter's contract, stated as a property rather than asserted on
    /// one example: if it rejects a pair, the full search provably could not
    /// have scored above the threshold.
    #[test]
    fn the_prefilter_is_a_genuine_bound() {
        let (u, g, k) = (Universe::generate(6), Geodesic::<42>::build().unwrap(), consts());
        let mut rng = borbax_rng::Stream::new(5, borbax_rng::Domain::Molecule, 0);
        let threshold = -40.0;
        for _ in 0..100_000 {
            let (mut a, mut b) = (Signature::<42>::zeroed(), Signature::<42>::zeroed());
            for i in 0..42 {
                a.r[i] = rng.next_f64_range(0.2, 2.5);
                b.r[i] = rng.next_f64_range(0.2, 2.5);
                a.a[i] = rng.next_f64_range(-1.0, 1.0);
                b.a[i] = rng.next_f64_range(-1.0, 1.0);
            }
            if !may_bind(&a, &b, &k, threshold) {
                assert!(affinity(&a, &b, &g, &k) < threshold, "pre-filter rejected a pair that scored above threshold");
            }
        }
        // And it must actually reject something, or it is not a filter.
        let a = real_signature::<42>(&g, &u);
        assert!(may_bind(&a, &complement_of(&a, &g, &k), &k, threshold));
    }

    #[test]
    fn bind_probability_is_monotone_in_affinity() {
        use borbax_units::Thermal;
        let t = Thermal(300.0);
        assert!(bind_probability(-1.0, t, -5.0) > bind_probability(-10.0, t, -5.0));
        assert!((0.0..=1.0).contains(&bind_probability(-5.0, t, -5.0)));
        // A perfect fit must be able to exceed even odds. Without the midpoint
        // the logistic saturates at 0.5 and nothing binds more readily.
        assert!(bind_probability(0.0, t, -5.0) > 0.5);
    }
}
```
